先週（10/26～10/30）発表の主な経済指標
AI関連を中心に設備投資が増勢を維持し、産業用機械にも広
がり。好調な企業収益も投資を後押し。個人消費も堅調。金
融所得や移転所得、株高による資産効果が購買力を下支え
政策要因を除く都区部CPIは上昇基調。財価格の上昇が目立
つ一方、サービス価格改定の広がりは限定的。企業の価格転嫁
は進展も、消費マインドの弱さが値上げの重荷に

のコアPCE物価は3％を超える伸びが継続。景気の
底堅さを背景にFRBにもう一段の金融引き締め余地

労働分配率は低下傾向。賃金
以外の所得が家計所得を補完
米国：企業収益が拡大する一方、労働分配率は低下。金融所得や移転所得が消費を下支え
 企業収益が好調を維持する一方、労働所得の対GDP比は低下傾向。金融所得や政府からの移転所得が家計所得を補完
― 株式配当や社会保障給付など、賃金以外の所得が家計の購買力を支える構図
 こうした所得環境に加え、株高による資産効果も消費を下支え。10月入り後も個人消費は底堅く推移
― 燃料高にもかかわらず、空港利用者数は前年並みの水準を維持し、小売売上高も高めの伸びを維持
― 年末商戦も前年を上回る伸びが予想されている

日本：コアCPIは10月に鈍化も、政策要因を除けばインフレ率は上昇基調
4
 10月の都区部コアＣＰＩは前年比＋2.6％（前月：同＋2.7％）と伸び鈍化
― 無償化期間の時期ズレによる9月水道料金の一時的な押し上げが剥落したことに加え、コメ価格の下落が影響
 ただし、政策要因を除けばインフレ率は上昇基調。サービス物価が底堅く推移する中、財物価の加速がインフレ率押し上げ
― 原油高等によるコスト増の転嫁が進展し、エネルギー、その他財（除く水道料金）の価格上昇が足元で加速
― 春先に鈍化した一般サービス物価（除く家賃）も人件費等の転嫁進展を映じて前年比＋2％近傍で推移

―10月は外食を中心に価格改定の動きがみられるも、広がりは限定的。一方、外食除く改定品目では値上げ幅が拡大
（注） 食品は、コメと外食を除く食料価格。水道料金はその他財に該当。サービスは一般
サービスと公共サービスの合計（家賃を除く）

日本：企業の価格転嫁は進展も、消費マインドの弱さが追加値上げの重荷に
5
 企業の価格転嫁は着実に進展
― 日商LOBO調査では、コスト増加分の4割以上を価格転嫁できた企業が55.4％と4月から2.6％ポイント増加。ただし、労
務費の転嫁は40.6％にとどまり、販売単価DIも小幅低下するなど、中小企業では追加値上げの勢いに一服感
 消費者態度指数は2カ月連続で悪化。消費マインドの弱さは残存
― 物価高による節約志向が企業の追加値上げを制約している可能性。日銀は基調的な物価上昇率が2％に到達したと判
断し、上振れリスクへの警戒を強化。ただし、サービス価格の改定に広がりはみられず
（出所） 日本商工会議所「LOBO調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（出所） 内閣府「消費動向調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 7

米国：10月FOMC～据え置き、ウォーシュ議長は引き締めがAIセクターにも影響との認識示す
6
 3.75～4.00％に据え置き（2人が反対、利上げを支持）。景気評価を上方修正しつつ、9月利上げ効果を見極め
― 景気評価は「堅調」から「力強い」へ。ウォーシュ議長は投資の広がりに言及
 AIセクターでは投資の選別が起きており、高い成長期待を持つ同セクターに対しても金融政策は有効との認識示す
 2027・2028年の会合の一部を政策決定を行わず、声明文も公表しない戦略的議論の会合とすると表明
AIセクター、生産性、タスクフォース声明文の変更、政策判断、会合運営
 反対2票は利上げを主張
 景気評価は「堅調」から「力強い」へ
 様子見による「情報のさらなる蓄積」
が、物価安定への「適時の復帰」のため
の政策判断に役立つ
声明文
 「以前より引き締まった」が「引き締め
的な水準にあるわけではない」と述べ、
追加利上げの余地残す
 「参加者の多数派は利上げ効果が出てい
ると認識」と言及

金融環境
と効果
 「政治は、この決定に何の役割も果たし
ていない」と言明 中間選挙
 2027年秋の1回、2028年には2回の会合
を政策決定せず議論だけの会合へ
 戦略的な熟議のための時間と余地確保
会合運営
 「投資家が期待する収益率はあまりに違
うので、AIには何の影響も及ばない」と
いう見方を否定
 「新技術と今回の投資急増」に対して金
融政策は有効
 信用スプレッドの拡大などは「市場参加
者が、どのような投資を行いたいか、資
本配分を選別している」ことの表れ
AI投資
 「経済全体の生産性は、ここ2、3年ほど
上向いて」おり、高い経済成⾧率によっ
てインフレ圧力が生まれ、利上げが必要
な状況ではないとの認識
生産性
11月16日に生産性・雇用の公聴会。タスク
フォース報告は年末が目安（一部は遅延の
可能性）。バランスシート提言は2027年1
～3月期の早期を想定

### Page 8

内容ポイント
 26年3Qの景気は引き続き拡大
 エネルギー価格と資金調達コストの上昇が今後の重しに景気
 エネルギー価格の見通しは9月の予想を上回っている
 今後数四半期の物価は目標を大幅に上回る見通し。その後
はエネルギー価格低下と金利上昇により目標水準に回帰へ
 物価の基調・賃金はおおむね安定。ただし、価格形成の初
期段階では上昇圧力がみられる
 今後6週間で得られる、基調的なインフレに関する多くの情
報を、とりわけ注視していく
物価
 9月理事会以降、金融環境は引き締まっている。金利は上昇
し、株価は下落している
 期待インフレの上昇やハイパースケーラーの社債発行等、
複合的な要因が金利を押し上げ。加えて、債務の多い加盟
国は固有の課題に直面
 市場の不当かつ無秩序な動向が金融政策伝達の妨げとな
る場合、対応手段は用意されている
金融
環境
 全会一致で金利据え置きも、利上げが適切かどうか議論し
た。「今利上げを実施してはどうか」との意見もあった
 今後の金融政策はデータに基づき、会合ごとに決定

金利水準 運転資金・在庫

（「増加」－「減少」、％）
欧州：ECBは政策金利を据え置き。高金利は企業の資金需要を押し下げ
7
 ECBは預金ファシリティ金利を2.5％で据え置き。利上げが議論されるも物価の基調に著変なく、慎重な対応が適切と判断
― 利上げの要否検討にあたり、エネルギー価格の上昇と金融環境の引き締まりが主な論点に。今後は物価の基調に関す
る指標を特に注視する姿勢。ラガルド総裁は、進むべき方向が「きわめて明確」であると発言
― ラガルド総裁は2027年2月に早期退任へ。同年5月までに専務理事2名を含む計3名の幹部が交代する見込み
 10月の銀行貸出調査によれば、金利水準が企業の資金需要減少に寄与。高金利の影響は既に一部で顕在化
― 同調査によれば、企業向け貸出基準はフランスで一段と厳格化。銀行のリスク許容度低下やリスク認識の高まりが主因

企業向け銀行貸出の需要増減要因
（注） 需要増減は、企業向け貸出の需要が前四半期から「かなり増加」「やや増加」と回
答した銀行の割合から、「かなり減少」「やや減少」と回答した銀行の割合を引いた
もの。「その他要因」には他の調達手段による代替や、「運転資金・在庫」「設備投
資」以外の資金需要発生等を含む。2026年Q3の調査期間は9月21日～10月6日
（出所）ECBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
10月ECB理事会のポイント（声明文・総裁会見）
（出所）ECBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
金利水準の高さが
貸出需要を下押し

### Page 9

英国：政府は財政規律を重視しつつエネルギー高対策に注力。財政余力は縮小
 英政府は秋季財政計画を公表。財政規律を重視しつつ家計や小規模事業者向け支援等を打ち出す内容
― 燃料税減税と電気料金へのVAT減税の延長、給付金支給によるエネルギー高対策、住宅購入支援などの家計支援策
を発表。小規模事業者の税負担軽減や電気料金の引き下げなど、企業向け支援も公表
― 上記政策の財源は銀行への追加課税やギャンブルへの課税強化等で確保する方針
― 防衛費は2030年までにGDP比3％へ引き上げる方針。ただし、財源を含む詳細は来年の歳出見直しに先送り
 財政余力は前回3月の237億ポンドから147億ポンドに大幅縮小。中東情勢悪化に伴う金利上昇等が背景
英国：財政余力（左）と3月以降の財政余力減少要因（右）
（出所）OBRより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
英国：秋季財政計画の主な内容
（出所） 英国政府より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
• 燃料税5ペンス減税や電気料金のVAT免除を来年7月まで実施
• 2,000万世帯を対象に100ポンドのエネルギー高支援給付金支給

• 使用量にかかわらず課される家庭用ガスのスタンディングチャー
ジ廃止
• エネルギー集約型製造業1万社の電気代を20～25％軽減
• 手ごろな住宅建設に15億ポンド追加拠出。住宅初回購入者向け
支援「Your First Home」に47億ポンドを充当
• 小規模事業者の事業用不動産税免除対象を拡大。パブや音楽会
場等には同税を追加で20％軽減
• 食品等100件以上の品目の関税撤廃
• 防衛投資計画で必要とされていた150億ポンドの追加投資全額を
拠出。2030年に防衛費GDP比3％達成を企図。来年の歳出見直
しで財源の詳細公表
• 地方分権の一環として、来年から事業用不動産税のうち20％を地
方に移転、2030年までに100％への引き上げを目指すほか、所得
税も2028年から一部を地方に配分しインフラ整備の財源に
• 銀行への上乗せ課税を3％から6％に引き上げ
• 高額賭博機へのギャンブル税を20％から25％に引き上げ
• 貯蓄・不動産所得の税率を2％Pt引き上げ

英国：財政懸念を背景とする金利上昇リスクはくすぶり続ける見通し
 財政計画がおおむね事前の予想どおりの内容であったことなどから英10年債利回りの動きは限定的。ただし、足元の高金
利の要因であるインフレや財政懸念、世界的なAI関連投資増加は今後も続くとみられ、金利は高止まりが予想される

 利払い費による財政圧迫継続が予想される中、財政懸念を背景とする一段の金利上昇リスクはくすぶり続ける見通し
― 高金利継続による利払い負担が財政を圧迫。財政を巡る不確実性が高い状況は続く見通し
― 欧州各国では財政懸念や政情不安を背景に高債務国中心に国債利回り上昇
― 財政への懸念を払拭しにくい状況が続くなか、英国でも金利急騰とこれに伴う財政悪化リスクには要注意
政府債務残高と10年債利回り変化幅の関係

中国：製造業利益は引き続きAI需要と資源高がけん引。7～9月期の利益率は前期を下回る
 9月の製造業総利益は前年比＋14.4％と前月（同＋9.1％）から加速。引き続きAI需要と資源高がけん引
― コンピューター・通信機器は同＋72.9％の増益。石油・石炭加工は単月赤字から一転し、同10倍超の伸び
― 他方、鉄鋼は単月赤字に転落したほか、自動車も同▲68.5％の減益。過剰生産業種は厳しい状況
 7～9月期の製造業の売上高利益率は5.0％と4～6月期（5.5％）から低下。自動車など幅広い業種で前期を下回る
― コンピューター・通信機器は7.4％で、前年（4.3％）から急速に改善。資源高で非鉄金属加工も好調

― 他方、自動車は2.5％と、前年（3.8％）から大幅に悪化。過当競争による販売価格の低下が響いた格好

― 韓国、台湾の主要な半導体・電子部品企業は、生産能力増強のため大規模な設備増強投資を計画

 中東情勢緊迫化後、食料や燃料を中心にインフレ率が急上昇したタイとフィリピンでは、4～6月期に消費の伸びが減速

― タイでは燃料を中心とする交通費、フィリピンでは交通関連に加え、宿泊飲食なども抑制する形で消費者が節約行動
 背景に実質賃金の鈍化による購買力の低下
― 購買力低下を受け、タイは6月～11月に消費支援策（食品・日用品などの購入補助）を実施、フィリピンは政府による企
業への補助も伴いながら首都圏の最低賃金の大幅な引き上げを6月に発令。物価上昇への対応が両国の政治課題に

日本：基調的物価は2％到達も安定化には距離。日銀は利上げの影響を見極め微妙な舵取りへ
13
日銀10月会合のポイント（展望レポート・総裁会見）
（出所）日本銀行「経済・物価情勢の展望」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
 日銀は基調的物価について「2％目標と概ね整合的な水準」に評価変更も、10月会合では利上げを見送り
― 今後は物価を2％で安定化させる局面へ。金融環境は緩和的との評価を維持し、次回12月会合で追加利上げの公算大
― 経済・物価はオントラックとの評価。原油価格の想定上振れを一部反映も見通し修正は軽微
― 総裁会見では「直近で物価上振れリスクが高まったわけではない」との発言。一段の利上げ加速は喫緊の必要性なし
 利上げを通じた基調的物価への影響を見極める手がかりとして、日銀は金融環境を注視

― 銀行・企業向けアンケート調査や銀行貸出残高にみられる変化、グローバル金融市場からの影響波及には要注意
経済・物価見通し
（注） 青字＝上昇修正、赤字＝下方修正。ドバイ原油価格は1バレル105ドル程度を出発
点に70ドル程度まで下落していくと前提。飲食料品消費税率の引き下げの影響は
織り込まず
（出所） 日本銀行より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
全体として修正は軽微。日銀見通し
対比でオントラックとの評価
展望レポート
• 基調的物価上昇率は2%物価目標と概ね整合的な水準
• 実質金利は上昇しているが、短期中心になお低水準。資金需要、
資産市場動向等も踏まえると金融環境は依然緩和した状態
• 名目賃金は前年並みのベースアップが実現するもとで、着実な増
加が続く見込み
• 海外の多くの中央銀行が利上げ方向に政策を転換。グローバルな
金融環境の変化がわが国の経済・物価に及ぼす影響に留意
会見中の総裁発言
• 物価上振れリスクはなおも無視できないが、1カ月前から物価上振
れリスクは大きく高まったわけではない

• 基調的物価が2%にアンカーされている度合いは極めて低い
• 金融政策が適時適切に発動され、緩和度合いを調整していくこと
で期待が安定化されていく
• 本支店ヒアリングでは、中小零細を中心に資金繰りがタイトになり
つつあるという声が聞かれ、今後の広がりを注意深くみていく
• 早めの政策対応を基本方針とするべき状況までは至っていない

日本：AI関連財の増産を背景に設備投資は今後拡大も、輸入依存の高さが成長の抑制要因に
14
 9月鉱工業生産は前月比＋2.2％と3カ月ぶりに増産。26年に入ってから増産基調を維持
― 増産の主因はAI関連。旺盛なAI関連需要を背景に、半導体製造装置などの増産が鉱工業生産をけん引
― 加えて、自動車生産の持ち直しなど、熊本地震や豪雨による下振れからの回復も生産の押し上げに寄与
 AI関連を中心に増産基調の生産動向は、今後の設備投資拡大を示唆
― ただし、資本財の輸入依存度は上昇傾向。AI関連業種で設備不足感が強まるなか、資本財の供給を輸入に頼る構造
― 人手不足などの供給制約で国内メーカーの増産が追いつかず、拡大する投資需要の一部が海外に流出の可能性も

（2026年10月2日発刊）

世界経済はどこへ向かうのか？中東危機やAI・レジリエンス関連の投資が
もたらす変化や、経済・金融市場の行方を展望
【ポイント①】
中東危機が⾧期化するなかでも、AI・レジリエンス関連の旺盛な投資需要に
支えられ、世界経済は3％台の成⾧ペースを維持
【ポイント②】
世界はインフレ・高金利の新常態へ。成⾧・負担の偏在が経済主体間の
「耐性格差」を拡大
【ポイント③】
海外主要中銀はインフレ警戒から利上げモードへ、日銀も物価上振れを警戒

## Document 087: 2026年11月投資月報

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年11月投資月報.pdf
Report source: Yuanta (投資月報)
Date: 未知日期
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本
身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
AI 資本支出牽動股市，債市關注 Fed 政策走向
2026 年 11 月
10 月全球主要股市普遍回落，主要受累於 AI 資本支出攀升與融資壓力引發科技股評價修正。以
美元計價的報酬來看，MSCI 新興亞洲下跌 8.6%最弱，新興市場跌 8.1%；其中韓國 KOSPI 單月重
挫 16.2%、台股跌 5.7%，反映市場重新評估 AI 投資回收效益，半導體股首當其衝。MSCI 拉丁美洲
則下跌 7.7%，因巴西總統魯拉連任後財政紀律疑慮升高。另一方面，MSCI 印度上漲 1.2%表現最佳，
受企業獲利與資本利得稅調降預期支撐。歐股則受惠於資金轉向醫療保健等防禦性類股，加上能源股

受油價上漲帶動，抵銷了銀行類股的跌勢，惟歐元貶值也壓縮了其以美元計價的表現。
聯準會 10 月雖維持利率不變，但全球債市轉由避險需求主導，AI 投資融資及信用疑慮促使資金
流向公債，美國 10 年期公債殖利率由 9 月底 5.29%降至 10 月底的 4.94%。全球主權債上漲 1.34%
居冠，新興美元債與全球投資級公司債分別上漲 1.26%、1.18%。惟信用利差擴大抵銷部分利率紅利，
聖路易 Fed 資料顯示，美國高收益債利差(ICE BofA US High Yield Index OAS)由 9 月底的 312 bps
升至 10/29 的 326bps，導致全球高收益債僅漲 0.50%，亞洲高收益債更下跌 0.95%。新興當地債
10 月下跌 0.64%，主要受到巴西選後財政疑慮及匯率波動拖累。
在股市方面，儘管 10 月科技股因 AI 資本支出與投資回收疑慮出現明顯修正，但全球企業獲利表
現仍具韌性。隨著 AI 基礎建設持續擴張，HBM、伺服器及先進製程等相關供應鏈仍具中長期成長動

能；然而，市場關注焦點已由資本支出規模轉向投資效益與自由現金流，企業獲利能否符合高度預期，
將成為影響股價表現的關鍵。整體而言，美國、台灣及日本等具備 AI 供應鏈與科技產業優勢的市場
仍值得關注，惟 11 月美國期中選舉結果、聯準會政策預期及能源價格變化，仍可能牽動市場風險偏
好，預期後續股市將呈現獲利基本面主導、產業與個股表現分化的格局。
就債市而言，10 月下旬避險買盤帶動美國 10 年期公債殖利率回落至約 4.94%，但聯準會政策方
向仍偏緊。10 月 FOMC 以 10 比 2 維持利率於 3.75%～4.00%，兩名反對者均主張升息 1 碼，市場
對 12 月升息的預期機率已逾七成，加上多位官員釋出鷹派訊號，短期殖利率仍有反彈風險。另一方
面，美國經濟成長具韌性，財政赤字及公債供給壓力亦可能限制長天期殖利率下行。投資級債方面，
雖具票息收益優勢，但 AI 企業融資與新債供給增加，仍可能使信用利差承壓。整體而言，中天期美
國公債及信用品質良好的投資級公司債，仍具中期收益吸引力，惟升息預期尚未消退，應審慎控管存

續期間風險，並留意通膨、聯準會政策及信用利差變化等因素。

美國股市：獲利成長支撐基本面，AI 投資回收與高利率牽動評價
10 月美股走勢受到大型科技股評價、通膨預期及企業財報交互影響，標普 500 指數在月內呈現
震盪，指數領漲產業亦輪動。月初 AI 需求支撐大型科技股；中旬隨能源價格上揚及美債殖利率波動，
部分高本益比股票面臨獲利了結。月底大型科技公司公布財報後，市場對高資本支出的疑慮升溫，科
技股賣壓擴大。10 月 26 日至 30 日標普 500 下跌 0.57%，其中通訊服務與資訊科技分別下跌 5.32%
及 2.03%，醫療保健、原物料及公用事業則分別上漲 3.12%、2.79%及 2.04%。資金由科技權值股

轉向防禦性及傳統產業，指數內部表現分化。
美國總經方面，10 月 S&P Global 綜合 PMI 初值維持擴張，企業活動具韌性，但房市等利率敏
感產業承壓。能源價格走高使通膨回落存在不確定性。聯準會於 10 月 28 日 FOMC 會議決議維持聯
邦資金利率於 3.75%至 4.00%，符合市場預期，並將經濟活動評估上修為強勁；兩名官員則支持升
息一碼，突顯決策層對物價壓力的看法仍有差異。主席於記者會指出，先前政策緊縮已逐步影響家庭
及企業的信用條件，資本配置也趨向審慎。會後升息預期與美債殖利率波動，牽動科技股評價。就業、
薪資及通膨走勢仍是政策關鍵。
2026 年第三季美股企業財報整體維持成長，資訊科技與能源為獲利預估上修的主要來源。10 月
初研究顯示，資訊科技產業第三季每股盈餘預估上修 3.5%，能源產業上修 18%，但原物料與部分消
費類股遭下調，產業間獲利趨勢並不一致。不過，市場對企業獲利的檢視逐步由營收及訂單成長，延
伸至設備折舊、資本支出、融資負擔及自由現金流。部分公司即使業績優於預期，仍因財測保守或現

金流偏弱而承壓。大型科技股價反應取決於資本支出與獲利轉換效率。相較過去單純以營收成長衡量
科技需求，市場開始檢視折舊負擔對淨利率的影響。
展望 11 月，美股企業獲利仍可提供中期支撐，但利率維持高檔、AI 相關評價偏高，以及政策不
確定性，可能使主要指數短期波動加劇。產業配置維持大型科技核心部位，優先考量具技術壁壘、穩
定客戶需求及自由現金流的企業；對資本支出增幅明顯高於營收成長、融資需求較高或估值已充分反
映成長預期的個股，則宜採取較保守的部位管理。半導體與雲端供應鏈仍須區分訂單能見度與投資回
收期。醫療保健及公用事業需求較穩，可能吸引防禦性資金。後續變數包括科技企業財測、通膨、就
業與期中選舉後政策方向。

歐洲股市：法國財政與能源風險升溫，留意殖利率對經濟與股市之影響
10 月泛歐指數震盪收高，較 9 月底 634.89 點上漲約 1.87%，為 6 月以來最佳單月表現。月初受
到油價攀升、歐洲公債殖利率走高及法國財政疑慮干擾，指數承壓，銀行與利率敏感類股表現偏弱；
隨後企業財報陸續公布，能源與國防相關企業獲利支撐市場反彈，醫療保健等防禦性產業亦受到青
睞，部分藥廠上調營運展望後股價走強。下旬指數一度升抵逾一個月高點，惟月底美國科技企業財報
引發 AI 投資回收疑慮，歐洲半導體設備與科技股跟隨下挫。10 月漲勢呈現產業輪動與個股財報分化。

歐元區經濟維持擴張但動能趨緩。第三季 GDP 初值季增 0.4%，低於第二季的 0.6%，年增 1.3%；
10 月綜合 PMI 初值由 9 月 53.1 降至 51.9，反映企業新訂單成長放慢，法國罷工、政治動盪及融資
成本上升亦壓抑商業活動。能源價格推升投入成本，企業轉嫁能力卻未同步增強，形成成長放緩與物
價壓力並存的局面，企業招聘步調也趨於保守。ECB 於 10 月 29 日維持政策利率不變，指出能源衝
擊可能同時提高通膨並抑制成長，且融資環境已較前次會議緊縮。總裁拉加德表示曾討論立即升息，
將等待更多數據，而法國公債風險溢酬升高亦可能限制政策操作空間。
企業獲利則是 10 月歐股表現的重要支撐。依 LSEG 截至 10 月 29 日彙整的 144 家已公布財報企
業及其餘公司預估，STOXX 600 第三季獲利年增率上修至 23.6%，接近第二季 23.9%的成長幅度，
營收預估年增 13.6%。能源類股受油價上揚與煉油利差改善帶動，獲利預估年增 139%，為整體成長
主要來源，且明顯高於其他產業；扣除能源後，其餘企業獲利成長約 9.8%，仍高於過去 16 季平均

7.1%。產業分化持續明顯，國防與部分科技設備商受惠訂單需求，ASML 與 Ericsson 公布的業績亦
反映設備支出韌性；反觀汽車、零售等非必需消費產業面臨需求疲弱，房地產業受高利率與融資成本
壓抑，市場對財測指引及現金流品質的要求提高。
展望 11 月，歐洲能源股短期受惠高油價及煉油利差，但獲利基期與地緣政治變動可能擴大股價
波動，宜避免將當季高成長率直接推算為全年可望維持相同成長動能；國防、電力設備及具全球競爭
力的工業企業，若訂單與定價能力維持，仍具相對優勢。金融股須區分銀行個別資本體質與法國主權
財政風險，並觀察公債利差變化對融資成本的傳導；房地產及高度依賴歐洲內需的循環性消費產業則
偏審慎。後續關注 ECB 十二月利率決策、能源通膨走勢、法國預算協商及企業財測修正，若殖利率
再度上升而獲利改善未能擴散，歐股評價上行空間將受限制，個股分化可能延續。

日本股市：日銀暫緩升息但確認物價達標，半導體回檔後聚焦財報與 12 月政策
10 月日本股市的觀察主軸，集中於日本銀行政策動向、通膨變化與 AI 投資帶動的出口成長。日
銀於 10 月 30 日維持政策利率 1.25%不變，惟投票比由 1 票支持升息、其餘委員主張維持利率，轉
為 5 比 4，顯示內部對進一步緊縮的支持增加。會後市場對升息時點的預期並未明顯改變，焦點仍集
中於 12 月。日銀展望報告指出，通膨大致符合 2%目標，並將 2027 財年核心 CPI 預估由 2.4%上修
至 2.6%。此外，ETF 年出售規模由 3,300 億日圓調降至 2,860 億日圓，反映政策正常化過程仍兼顧

市場承受能力，整體呈現利率暫停調整、緊縮基調延續的格局。
基本面方面，日本 9 月出口年增 16.7%，雖低於 8 月的 19.3%，仍連續第 13 個月成長，顯示外
需維持韌性。半導體等電子零組件、汽車及半導體製造設備出口，分別成長 49.4%、15.1%及 43.9%，
其中 AI 相關需求成為重要支柱。依出口地區觀察，對美出口年增 22.9%，半導體製造設備更大增
93.1%；對亞洲出口成長 17.5%，亦提供主要支撐。相較之下，對中國出口增幅放緩至 8.6%，主因
汽車出口年減 59.8%，顯示不同市場與產業的表現仍有落差。整體而言，出口成長並非全面同步擴張，
而是由 AI 投資與半導體設備需求帶動，後續仍須觀察成長動能能否延伸至更多產業。
展望 11 月，日本股市可聚焦通膨走勢、日銀政策訊號，以及 AI 資本支出的延續性。9 月 CPI 年
增率由 1.9%升至 2.1%，若加回能源政策約 0.82 個百分點的壓抑效果，實質漲幅達 2.9%，加上食品
與外食價格仍有上漲壓力，支持日銀維持偏緊縮立場。若後續數據持續反映物價壓力，12 月升息預

期可能成為影響市場評價的重要因素。產業方面，全球 AI 基礎建設與半導體資本支出若持續擴張，
日本半導體設備及電子零組件出口可望獲得支撐；韓國 10 月上旬半導體出口大幅成長，也提供區域
需求的觀察線索。不過，對中出口放緩及汽車需求變化，仍是評估外需時不可忽略的風險。
整體而言，AI 投資熱潮與出口成長韌性，為日本股市後續表現帶來正向契機。儘管日銀政策正常
化仍是市場關注焦點，但全球 AI 基礎建設持續推進，有望支撐日本半導體設備及電子零組件需求，
並為相關企業帶來營運成長機會。可持續聚焦出口擴張與科技投資所帶動的基本面改善。若需求動能
延續並轉化為獲利成長，日本股市可望在政策調整過程中，持續展現結構性成長題材的吸引力。

台灣股市：外銷訂單連四月創高，台積電上修展望，市場料更重視獲利驗證
10 月台股基本面持續交出亮眼成績。經濟部公布，9 月外銷訂單達 1,142.1 億美元，月增 10.9%、
年增 62.4%，連續第四個月創單月新高，且已連續兩個月突破千億美元；第三季外銷訂單 3,151.0 億
美元，年增 65.0%，累計前三季 8,192 億美元，已超越去年全年的 7,437 億美元。貨品別方面，資
訊通信與電子產品分別達 438.8 億及 474.2 億美元，年增 87.3%及 71.0%，皆創單月新高；地區別
方面，美國訂單 453.8 億美元、年增 79.4%，主要來自伺服器、手機及筆電。企業端，台積電第三

季營收 467.2 億美元，季增 16.2%並優於財測高標，第四季營收預估 523 億至 543 億美元，全年美
元營收成長上修至約 44%，顯示 AI 需求仍未降溫。
對照廠商對 9 月接單的預期與實際結果，可發現 AI 需求向供應鏈擴散的判斷大致獲得驗證。當
時資訊通信與電子產品的訂單金額動向指數分別為 52.4 及 51.5，實際接單即雙雙創下單月新高；基
本金屬與化學品的動向指數高達 59.0 及 61.0，9 月接單亦分別年增 29.7%及 22.8%，其中基本金屬
受惠 AI 供應鏈帶動銅箔、銅箔基板需求增加。機械指數雖僅 46.3，但在半導體業者擴大資本支出帶
動下，9 月接單仍年增 21.5%並創同月新高。與 AI 供應鏈相關的傳統貨品表現較佳，但部分傳產仍
面臨供給過剩壓力，產業並非全面同步復甦。
展望 11 月，台股將在強勁基本面與外部評價調整之間拉鋸。10 月南韓股市因記憶體競爭與 AI
投資疑慮重挫，台股相對抗跌，加權指數月中一度再創歷史新高，但 10/15 及 10/30 仍分別出現 3.41%

與 2.49%的單日跌幅，顯示外資對 AI 題材的獲利了結賣壓仍在。經濟部預估 10 月外銷訂單將介於
1,125 億至 1,159 億美元，年增 62%至 67%，按金額計算的動向指數為 50.5，若高於 9 月的 1,142.1
億美元，將連續第五個月創單月新高。台積電亦重申 2026 年資本支出 600 億至 640 億美元，並表
示 2026 至 2028 年支出將顯著高於前三年，設備、廠務及先進封裝供應鏈的訂單能見度仍高。不過，
台積電也提醒消費性與價格敏感市場正面臨零組件漲價與總經不確定性，對 2027 年規劃採審慎態度。
整體而言，11 月維持偏正面的台股觀點，以重視獲利驗證作為配置方向。核心部位可聚焦晶圓
代工、先進封裝、伺服器代工及 ASIC 等需求明確且具定價能力的企業；擴散機會則可從銅箔基板、
高階材料、設備與廠務工程中，篩選已取得實際訂單、營收與毛利率同步成長的公司。對於評價已高、
主要依賴題材想像的個股，宜適度觀察，避免在市場重新檢視 AI 投入回收時承受較大波動。留意 11

月中下旬公布的 10 月外銷訂單、美國大型科技業者資本支出指引及輝達財報對市場情緒的影響。

中國股市：外需撐起生產，內需與房市仍疲弱，政策加碼成為關鍵
中國第三季經濟表現略優於預期。國家統計局公布，第三季 GDP 年增 4.5%，高於第二季的 4.3%，
前三季累計成長 4.6%，仍落在政府全年 4.5%至 5.0%的目標區間內。成長動能主要來自供給端與外
需：第三季製造業增加值年增 5.9%，資訊傳輸、軟體與資訊技術服務業年增 9.8%；前三季貨物進出
口年增 18.8%，受惠全球 AI 開發加速與電動車需求擴大，高科技產品出口表現強勁。另一方面，受
中東情勢推升原油與原物料價格影響，9 月 PPI 年增 5.0%，為今年以來最大漲幅，上游資源類企業

的獲利有望改善，但中下游製造業則面臨成本轉嫁壓力。
然而，從圖中可見，生產面與需求面的落差仍大。9 月工業生產年增 5.5%，服務業生產指數年
增 4.8%，兩者維持溫和成長；但房地產仍是經濟最大拖累，9 月房地產投資年減 26.2%，跌幅較 8
月的 25.4%進一步擴大。銷售端同步轉弱，銷售面積與銷售額分別年減 20.5%及 20.0%，較 8 月的
14.7%及 12.1%明顯惡化；竣工面積跌幅更由 28.4%擴大至 36.4%。新開工跌幅雖由 31.0%收斂至
25.6%，在建面積則持平於年減 12.8%，顯示開發商仍以去化庫存、控制投資為主，房市尚未出現止
穩訊號，並持續壓抑家庭財富效果與地方政府的土地財政收入。
內需疲弱是當前中國經濟的核心課題。9 月社會消費品零售總額僅年增 1.1%，汽車與家電等耐
用品銷售停滯，以舊換新補貼效果逐漸遞減；前三季固定資產投資年減 7.2%，基建與工業投資同步
下滑，第三季建築業增加值亦年減 4.4%。統計局也坦言國內需求不足的問題仍然突出。政策方面，7

月中央政治局會議定調下半年實施更積極的財政政策與適度寬鬆的貨幣政策，並適時推出增量政策；
10 月下旬召開的二十屆五中全會則以黨建議題為主。市場表現上，10/30 上證綜合指數受半導體股
拖累下跌 1.44%，香港恒生指數則在必需消費與能源股帶動下上漲 1.46%，資金明顯偏好防禦型內
需與高股息族群。
整體而言，對中國股市宜維持中性偏審慎的態度。經濟成長雖守住目標區間，但房地產投資與銷
售跌幅擴大、零售成長放緩，內需尚未出現明確回升訊號，在增量刺激政策落地前，指數大幅走高的
條件仍不充分。配置上可聚焦兩條主線：一是受惠出口與產業升級的高端製造、資訊科技服務與國產
算力供應鏈，但須留意資本支出擴張對現金流的壓力；二是具穩定配息與現金流的高股息、公用事業
及必需消費族群，在內需疲弱時提供防禦。後續可觀察年底中央經濟工作會議對財政擴張與房市支持
的力度，作為調整布局的依據。

拉美市場：魯拉意外連任引發財政紀律疑慮，巴西股市重挫
10 月拉美市場走勢主要受巴西總統大選左右。月初弗拉維奧・波索納洛意外在首輪總統選舉中
領先，提高市場對政黨輪替與財政改革的期待，帶動巴西股市同步走強；但 10 月 25 日現任總統
魯拉在決選中仍以 51.79%的得票率勝出，市場原先的期待落空，轉而擔憂巴西財政赤字可能進一步
惡化，巴西資產因此遭到拋售。由於巴西約占 MSCI 拉丁美洲指數約六成權重，巴西股市下跌使拉
美市場整體出現明顯回落，後續巴西股市能否回穩，仍要觀察魯拉新任期提出的具體財政政策。
市場之所以對魯拉的財政政策如此敏感，主要是因為巴西的債務負擔仍在持續增加。高盛預估，

巴西一般政府總債務占國內生產毛額的比率，將由 2025 年的 78.6%升至 2026 年底的 83.5%。然
而，巴西部分支出不易削減，既有稅負又偏高，使政府調整收支的空間相當有限。在此情況下，高盛
預期魯拉新任期可能僅能溫和抑制不含利息的政府支出成長，並將依賴增加稅收來改善收支，推動大
幅結構性改革的可能性則相對較低。若巴西財政調整最終以增稅為主，而支出未能有效控制，市場不
僅會擔心巴西財政赤字難以縮小，也會擔心新增稅額侵蝕企業獲利。
此外，即使魯拉政府提出新的財政改革方案，仍可能受制於政治立場相左的新任國會。在本次國
會改選後，右派與中間偏右政黨合計將掌握眾議院約 59%、參議院約 64%的席次，弗拉維奧所屬的
自由黨也成為參眾兩院的最大黨，因此魯拉推動預算與改革時，必須爭取政治立場不同的議員支持。
高盛報告指出，儘管巴西國會中間政黨仍可能與魯拉政府合作，但可能以內閣職位、預算分配及國營
企業人事作為交換條件。這樣的協商雖有助魯拉政府在國會取得多數支持，卻也可能使原有方案遭到
修改，進而影響巴西財政整頓的幅度與速度。

巴西財政政策的變化，最終會透過利率與匯率影響股市。巴西現行 Selic 政策利率為 13.75%，
選前市場原本預期巴西央行將延續降息步調，但選後巴西里爾急貶，進口成本上升恐進一步推升通
膨，限制巴西央行降息空間，在利率條件緊縮的狀況下，也將使仰賴國內需求的企業獲利受到壓抑。
不過，儘管巴西財政政策仍有變數，巴西經濟本身仍具備一定的支撐條件。高盛報告指出，巴西
農業具國際競爭力，油氣產量持續成長，並在綠色能源與關鍵礦物領域具有發展潛力；此外，巴西的
貿易順差、外國直接投資與充裕外匯存底，也能為外部資金波動提供緩衝，持續為巴西經濟提供支撐。
整體而言，巴西的產業優勢與外部收支條件，使巴西經濟基本面仍具支撐。然而，魯拉新任期的
財政部長人選、2027 年預算安排，以及魯拉政府與國會的協商進展，將左右市場對巴西財政與降息
的預期，在財政政策方向明朗之前，短線拉美市場波動恐將加劇。

科技類股：CSP 資本支出續增壓縮現金流，AI 受惠族群走向分化
10 月下旬美國大型科技業者第三季財報，讓市場對 AI 的關注由投入多少轉向何時回收。營運面
普遍不差：Meta 第三季營收 646 億美元、年增 26%，廣告單價年增 14%，顯示 AI 精準投放已轉化
為實際收入；Alphabet 旗下 Google Cloud 營收年增 103%至 307.1 億美元。然而，Alphabet 搜尋
營收成長放緩至 12%，加上自由現金流為負 90.7 億美元、遠差於市場預期，股價於 10/29 單日重挫
12.6%，創上市以來最大單日跌幅；Meta 自由現金流亦轉為負 21.2 億美元。相較之下，微軟資本支

出低於預期、自由現金流維持正值。市場並非否定 AI 需求，而是開始以現金流紀律區分企業。
從四大雲端服務業者（CSP）最新一季的營業現金流與資本支出，可清楚看出壓力所在。Amazon
資本支出約 759 億美元，遠高於約 424 億美元的營業現金流，缺口最大；Alphabet 資本支出約 514
億美元，同樣超過約 423 億美元的營業現金流；Meta 資本支出約 349 億美元，亦略高於約 328 億
美元的營業現金流。四家業者中，僅微軟營業現金流約 580 億美元，明顯高於約 394 億美元的資本
支出。這意味多數 CSP 已需動用既有現金或對外融資支應 AI 建置，投資節奏將更受資金成本與股東
回報要求牽制；但從另一面看，各業者全年資本支出指引並未下修，Meta 與 Alphabet 分別維持 1,350
億至 1,450 億美元及 1,950 億至 2,050 億美元的區間，對上游供應鏈而言，短期訂單能見度仍高。
依 AI 需求能見度與財務韌性兩個面向劃分科技子產業版圖來看。核心受惠區的大型 CSP、半導

體設備、CPU／ASIC 及高階光通訊，兼具明確訂單與穩健財務，例如 Lumentum 最新一季營收年增
109%、非 GAAP 毛利率突破 50%，並指出部分雷射元件供給仍吃緊。穩健型的消費電子、手機晶
片與類比 IC，AI 需求能見度較低，但財務結構穩定，可在科技股波動時發揮防禦作用；伺服器整機
與組裝雖受惠出貨成長，卻面臨毛利偏薄、零組件成本與能源成本上升的風險，股價波動較大。至於
高槓桿的 AI 算力出租業者，在利率居高與信用利差擴大下最為脆弱，10 月 CoreWeave 債券價格下
跌即引發市場對 AI 信用風險的擔憂，成為本波修正的導火線之一。
整體而言，科技股以「核心基本面受惠為主、穩健型為輔」來觀察。可聚焦半導體設備、CPU
／ASIC 及高階光通訊等兼具訂單能見度與財務韌性的族群，並以 11 月指標性廠商 Lumentum、輝
達等財報驗證需求延續性；大型 CSP 中，則可優先選擇營業現金流足以支應資本支出、投資節奏較
具紀律的業者。穩健型的消費電子與手機晶片等，可作為降低整體波動的衛星配置。伺服器整機與組

裝族群宜觀察具議價能力者，避免在零組件與能源成本上升時承受毛利壓力。AI 長期趨勢未變，但
市場已進入用現金流與業績說話的階段，選股應更重視需求能否轉化為獲利，對科技股維持正向看法。

能源類股：柴油短缺支撐油價，但若中東衝突緩解，明年原油有望轉為供過於求
10 月西德州原油價格隨荷莫茲海峽油輪與沙烏地阿拉伯能源設施持續遇襲，整體走勢仍呈現震
盪走高。展望 11 月，偏低的能源庫存與冬季需求預期仍有利油價支撐；然而，國際能源署（IEA）、
美國能源資訊署（EIA）與石油輸出國組織（OPEC）等 3 大能源機構於 10 月月報同步下修今年石
油需求預估，且 IEA 與 EIA 均預期明年供給增幅將遠高於需求，故若後續中東局勢出現好轉，石油
供應緊張情況也將有望逐步緩解。
3 大能源機構在 10 月月報均下修今年需求預估，IEA 將 2026 年全球石油需求由年減每日

250 萬桶調整為年減 260 萬桶，EIA 由年減約 170 萬桶調整為年減約 200 萬桶，OPEC 則由年
增每日 38 萬桶下修至 18 萬桶，預估分歧主要來自中國、中東等非 OECD 國家的需求預估。
展望明年，IEA 與 EIA 均預期石油市場將轉趨寬鬆。IEA 預估 2027 年全球供給將較今年增加
每日 770 萬桶，需求僅增加 200 萬桶；EIA 則預估供給增加約每日 850 萬桶，需求僅增加約 220
萬桶，顯示若後續中東受阻產能能夠恢復，供應回升速度將快於消費。OPEC 的預估則相對看好油
價，OPEC 將 2027 年需求由 9 月預估的每日增加 236 萬桶上調至增加 255 萬桶，並預估市場
需要 OPEC+ 提供的原油將由今年的每日 4,210 萬桶至明年增至 4,390 萬桶，換言之，OPEC 認
為明年需求成長將超過其他產油國的供給增幅，OPEC+須再增產量才能滿足市場需求。
然而，即使中東衝突後續轉趨緩解，明年原油供需可能轉趨寬鬆，成品油供應仍可能維持緊俏。

IEA 指出，9 月全球柴油出口較去年同期減少約每日 70 萬桶，主因是中東成品油運輸受阻與俄羅
斯煉油設施受損，加上秋季煉油廠進入歲修，使市場難以在入冬前補足柴油庫存。因此，IEA 認為即
使 2027 年第一季全球石油市場恢復供過於求，較明顯的庫存回補仍可能要等到明年下半年。由於
成品油供應偏緊，EIA 雖然下修石油需求預測，卻仍將 2026 年西德州原油均價預測由每桶 84.65
美元上調至 88.21 美元，2027 年則由 69.74 美元上調至 79.74 美元。
另一方面，各國政策措施可望在過渡期間持續提供供應緩衝。G7 於 10 月 2 日承諾在四個月
內協調釋出 1 億桶原油與油品，並優先供應柴油；美國放寬俄羅斯柴油交易限制後，美聯社引述船
運追蹤機構 Kpler 指出，首艘載運約 4 萬噸柴油的油輪已經啟航，預計 11 月 7 日抵達紐約，均
有利於緩解短期成品油短缺問題。整體而言，偏低的成品油庫存與中東航運風險，短期仍有利支撐油
價，然而在三大機構均下修今年石油需求預估，且 IEA 與 EIA 預期明年供給增幅將明顯高於需求的情

況下，後續油價進一步大幅上漲空間也相對有限。

身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元大銀行所有，非經本行同意，
不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕不另行通知。
黃金類股：中國及全球央行買盤仍穩健，然短線市場資金轉向流出
近期金價走勢仍未擺脫偏弱格局，主因是中東能源供應風險推升油價，市場擔憂通膨難以迅速降
溫，並持續評估聯準會於今明兩年再度升息，而利率維持高檔將提高持有黃金的機會成本。此外，
10 月中下旬開始部分避險資金轉向美國公債，北美黃金 ETF 也出現資金撤出，使金價即使在美元
與美債殖利率出現回落的情況下，仍缺乏買盤支撐。然而，中長線來看，全球央行購金的趨勢目前
仍未出現明顯轉向，加上中國投資買盤持續流入，均有助支撐中長期黃金需求。
從黃金 ETF 資金來看，黃金投資需求在 10 月下旬由全面流入轉為地區分化。世界黃金協會資

料顯示，9 月全球實物黃金 ETF 淨流入 100 億美元，持有量增加 67 噸至 4,256 噸，創下持有
量歷史新高；第三季累計淨流入 310 億美元，也創下單季新高紀錄，然而，截至 10 月 23 日的
一週，全球黃金 ETF 轉為淨流出約 12 億美元，持有量減少 7.7 噸，其中北美黃金 ETF 淨流出
16.9 億美元，顯示隨金價再度向下破底，ETF 資金也開始轉趨保守。
然而另一方面，中國投資需求仍持續為金價提供支撐。世界黃金協會指出，中國黃金 ETF 9 月
淨流入人民幣 110 億元，持有量增加 11 噸至 304 噸，創下月底新高，且截至 10 月 23 日買
盤仍在延續。世界黃金協會指出中國股市表現偏弱，提高了當地投資人分散資產的需求，而中國公
債殖利率下降，也降低了持有黃金的機會成本，雙雙有利於中國資金流入黃金市場。
此外，央行購金仍是金價的結構性支撐。世界黃金協會統計，全球央行 8 月申報的淨購金量為
39 噸；另中國人民銀行 9 月增持 23 噸，為其連續第 23 個月增持黃金配置，波蘭央行年初至 8

月也累計購入 98 噸，使其總持有量達 648 噸，逐步接近波蘭央行設下的 700 噸目標。另一方面，
由於許多央行購金並未公開申報，高盛以倫敦金庫黃金流出量與英國黃金淨出口之間的差額，推估
未申報的央行買盤，估計全球央行 8 月實際購金量約 79 噸，經季節調整的三個月平均約每月 85
噸，遠高於 2022 年以前平均每月約 17 噸的水準。高盛報告指出，新興市場央行的黃金配置比重
仍低於長期合理水準，加上各國仍有動機分散持有七大工業國（G7）貨幣資產，因此預估在未來央
行買盤仍可望延續。
但仍需注意的是，也有個別央行開始調整黃金配置。哈薩克央行於 10 月中旬表示，由於 10 年
期美債殖利率升至 24 年高點，提高了美元資產的吸引力，故計畫在 2026 年底前，將黃金占儲備
資產的比重由約 78% 降至 75%。以哈薩克央行 386 噸的黃金持有量估算，此次調整規模約相當於
15 噸。雖然哈薩克央行的調整規模不大，但若其他黃金占比偏高的央行也跟進再平衡，全球央行需
求對金價的支撐力道可能因此減弱。

整體而言，全球央行購金趨勢尚未出現明顯轉向，加上中國投資買盤，均有助支撐中長期黃金需
求。然而，現階段高漲的通膨與升息預期，加上短線 ETF 資金撤出，仍可能使短期金價面臨震盪。

任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行及本行之任何董事或受僱人，毋須負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本

固定收益：避險需求支撐美債，聚焦中期美債及投資級債
10 月美國公債殖利率呈現月初攀升、月中震盪回落、月底再度下行的走勢。月初受能源價格上
漲、通膨預期升溫，以及 AI 投資帶動經濟成長與資金需求增加影響，10 年期美債殖利率於 10 月 5
日升至 5.31%，30 年期升至 5.66%。隨後市場對 AI 基礎建設融資與企業現金流的疑慮升高，科技股
賣壓帶動資金轉向公債，10 年期殖利率於 10 月 15 日降至 5.10%；22 日大型科技股重挫再度推升
避險需求，進一步降至 4.96%。月底雖然美國經濟維持韌性、聯準會於 28 日維持利率不變，通膨及

財政赤字仍限制長天期利率下行空間，但科技企業資本支出疑慮持續支撐債市。
美國經濟呈現就業成長放緩、通膨壓力仍高的局面。9 月非農就業僅增加 2.9 萬人，失業率由 4.1%
升至 4.2%，反映企業聘僱動能趨緩；惟通膨尚未明顯改善，9 月 CPI 年增 3.7%，月增 0.6%，主要
受能源價格上漲帶動，核心 CPI 年增 2.6%。聯準會偏好的核心 PCE 物價指數年增 3.1%，仍高於 2%
目標。Fed 於 10 月 28 日以 10 比 2 票維持政策利率於 3.75%至 4.00%不變，並將經濟活動評估由
「穩健」上調至「強勁」，指出消費支出具韌性、企業資本投資維持強勁，兩位官員則因通膨疑慮主
張升息一碼。Fed 強調將等待更多經濟數據再決定政策方向，未明確承諾後續升息時點，顯示就業放
緩與通膨黏著仍是政策判斷的主要拉鋸因素。
信用市場方面，十月中旬 CoreWeave 公司債價格重挫，凸顯人工智慧基礎建設高度仰賴外部融
資的脆弱環節。根據路透報導，公司債務規模約五百億美元，部分債券殖利率一度攀升至百分之十五

至十六，五年期信用違約交換利差突破一千一百個基點，顯示投資人要求的違約風險補償大幅提高。
疑慮集中於建置成本、設備折舊、客戶收款與債務支出的時間差，以及再融資成本。即使需求強勁，
合約收入仍不等同即時現金流，建置延宕可能加重償債壓力。相關賣壓亦使投資人重新區分大型科技
企業與高度槓桿資料中心營運商的信用體質。此事件尚不代表整體投資級信用惡化。
展望十一月，美債配置建議仍以中期公債與優質美元投資級公司債為核心，兼顧較高票息水準與
政策預期反覆下的存續期間風險。十年期殖利率回落至百分之五附近後，中天期債券仍較超長天期不
易受期限溢酬波動干擾。投資級公司債可優先篩選利息保障倍數充足、現金流穩健及再融資需求可控
的發行人，避免僅憑信用評等忽略產業資本支出周期。高收益債須檢視利差補償，尤其資料中心借款
人的抵押品與客戶集中度。後續關注十一月美國期中選舉、就業與通膨數據、財政部發債規畫及聯準
會十二月會議預期，並追蹤信用利差是否由個別高槓桿企業擴散至整體市場。

3Q26   margin miss from product

3Q26 earnings missed our estimate by 13% and consensus by 16%, given less
favorable product mix...   There was less  inspection (gross margin >60%) but more
automation tools (gross margin ~40%).
...as well as weaker subsidiary company performance.
Looking into 4Q26, we expect gross margin to recover to >50% from improving
product mix: However, this will still be slightly offset by new product shipped to

the OSATs, which carries lower margin in the early ramping phase.
We think CoWoS should still account for 70-80% of 2027e total revenue – with
other new customers and tools, including a US customer, CPO, and CoW process.
We think CPO could account for 15-20% of 2027e revenue, with shipments up >10x
from 2026.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -

SK하이닉스 (000660) 
높아지는 이익의 눈높이, 달라지는 메모리 사이클

Buy(유지) 
목표주가 3,150,000원, 현재 주가(10/28): 1,487,000원 
▶ Analyst 박준영 jyp94@hanwha.com 3772-7481 / RA 김나우 now.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7710

3Q26 Review: HBM4 출하 확대와 예상보다 강한 가격 상승 
분기 매출액 104.6조원(QoQ +32%, YoY +328%), 영업이익 83.3조원(QoQ +38%, YoY +632%)을 기록하며 시장 기대
치를 상회함. 영업이익률은 전분기 대비 약 3%p 상승한 80%로 사상 최대 분기 실적을 달성함. 특히 DRAM ASP가 전
분기 대비 20% 후반 상승하며 당사 기존 예상치인 +23%를 상회한 점에 주목할 필요가 있음. NAND ASP 역시 20% 
중반 상승하며 예상보다 강한 가격 흐름을 나타냄. DRAM ASP 상승에는 기존 DRAM의 가격 인상과 함께 3분기부터 
본격화된 HBM4 양산 및 출하 확대에 따른 제품 믹스 개선 효과가 반영됨. 서버 DDR5 판매량 역시 전분기 대비 40% 
증가하며 실적 성장을 뒷받침함. 특히 HBM4는 기존 HBM3E 대비 높은 기술적 난도를 반영한 가격으로 공급되고 있어 
향후 출하 비중 확대에 따른 추가적인 ASP 상승 효과도 기대됨. 
이번 실적에서 중요한 부분은 예상보다 높은 가격 상승폭이 2027년 실적 추정치의 상향 가능성을 높이고 있다는 점임. 3
분기 DRAM ASP가 기존 추정치를 상회한 가운데 4분기에도 HBM4 출하 확대와 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 이어질 
것으로 예상되기 때문임. 동사는 4분기 DRAM 출하량이 전분기 대비 20% 중반 증가할 것으로 전망했으며, HBM4 출
하량이 HBM3E를 넘어 주력 제품으로 자리 잡을 것으로 예상함. 특히 HBM4의 양산 수율이 당초 목표를 상회하고 있
으며 높은 고객 수요에 대응하기 위해 생산성 극대화에 집중하고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. HBM4 출하 확대에 따른 믹스 
개선과 기존 DRAM 가격 상승이 동시에 진행되는 가운데 HBM과 일반 DRAM의 수익성 격차 역시 유의미하게 축소
될 것으로 예상됨. 이는 특정 제품에 대한 수익성 의존도가 낮아지면서 DRAM 사업 전반의 이익 체력이 강화되고 있음
을 의미함. 메모리 산업의 높은 영업 레버리지를 고려하면 예상보다 높은 ASP와 출하량 증가는 2027년 이익 추정치의 
추가 상향으로 연결될 가능성이 높다고 판단함.

공급 부족의 장기화와 LTA 확대, 메모리 산업의 이익 구조 변화 
이번 컨퍼런스콜에서 확인된 또 다른 중요한 변화는 공급 부족의 장기화와 장기공급계약(LTA) 확대임. 동사는 첨단 공정
의 기술적 난도 상승과 신규 생산시설 구축에 소요되는 시간을 고려할 때 메모리 공급이 수요를 충족하지 못하는 환경이 
2030년까지 지속될 것으로 전망함. 특히 글로벌 주요 고객사 14곳과 장기공급계약을 체결했으며, 2027~2028년 전체 생
산능력의 50% 이상에 해당하는 물량을 확보함. HBM의 경우 2029년까지의 물량이 이미 판매 확정된 상황으로 중장기 
수요 가시성이 상당히 높아졌다고 판단함. 주목할 부분은 LTA가 단순한 공급물량 확보를 넘어 메모리 업체의 사업 구조
와 이익 안정성을 변화시키고 있다는 점임. 계약 가격은 고객 및 제품별로 차이가 있으나 합의된 범위 내에서 시장 상황
을 반영하는 구조로 운영되고 있으며 예치금 등 실질적인 계약 이행력을 높이는 장치도 포함됨. 이는 장기적인 판매물량
을 확보하면서도 업황 상승에 따른 가격 인상 효과를 일정 부분 누릴 수 있다는 점에서 긍정적임.

### Page 2

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

2 
특히 전체 생산능력의 50% 이상을 LTA로 확보하면서 나머지 물량은 시장 상황에 따라 탄력적으로 대응할 수 있다는 점
에 주목할 필요가 있음. 장기계약을 통해 안정적인 매출 기반을 확보하는 동시에 미계약 물량을 활용해 추가적인 가격 
상승에 대응할 수 있기 때문임. 물론 장기계약이 메모리 가격 하락을 완전히 방어하는 것은 아니지만, 과거와 비교해 물
량 가시성과 고객사와의 협상력이 동시에 개선되고 있다는 점은 분명함. 과거 메모리 산업이 높은 이익을 창출하는 국면
에서도 낮은 밸류에이션을 적용 받았던 이유 중 하나는 이익의 지속성에 대한 불확실성이었음. 그러나 장기계약 확대와 
공급 제약의 장기화가 동시에 진행된다면 시장이 평가하는 이익의 지속성 역시 높아질 수 있음. 따라서 향후 주가 상승
은 단순한 EPS 추정치 상향뿐 아니라 이익 변동성 축소에 따른 밸류에이션 재평가가 동시에 진행되는 형태로 나타날 가
능성이 높다고 판단함. 
AI 인프라 투자에 대한 우려 역시 중장기 메모리 수요를 훼손할 가능성은 제한적이라고 판단함. 동사는 주요 하이퍼스케
일러의 클라우드 매출이 전년 대비 30~80% 성장하고 있으며 기업용 AI 솔루션 계약 확대와 클라우드 수주잔고 증가를 
통해 AI 투자의 사업적 성과가 확인되고 있다고 설명함. 특히 초기 AI 투자가 소수 빅테크에 집중되었던 것과 달리 최근
에는 일반기업의 자체 인프라 구축, 소버린 AI, 온프레미스 등으로 투자 주체가 다변화되고 있다는 점도 긍정적임. 특정 
사업자의 투자 속도 조절이 전체 AI 인프라 수요에 미치는 영향은 과거 대비 완화될 수 있기 때문임. AI 투자에 대한 우
려가 지속되는 상황에서도 실제 메모리 수요와 고객사들의 장기계약 확대가 확인되고 있다는 점에서 메모리 업황의 구조
적 성장에 대한 긍정적인 시각을 유지함.

HBM4 경쟁력 강화와 CAPEX 확대, 이익 성장과 주주환원의 선순환 
HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대와 신규 생산능력 확보 역시 2027년 실적 성장의 중요한 기반이 될 전망임. 동사는 M15X
의 생산능력을 연말까지 최대 수준으로 확대할 계획이며 HBM4의 양산 수율 역시 당초 목표를 상회하는 등 안정적인 
생산체계를 확보하고 있음. 특히 HBM 수요가 공급능력의 두 배를 상회하는 상황에서 고객사들의 높은 선호가 이어지고 
있다는 점은 경쟁력 유지에 긍정적임. 최근 일부 고객사들이 HBM 적층 단수를 낮추는 방안을 검토하고 있으나 이는 공
급 부족 상황에서 제한된 물량을 효율적으로 활용하기 위한 전략으로 판단됨. 적층 단수 조정이 전체 HBM 수요 감소로 
직결되는 것은 아니며 오히려 후공정 생산능력에 대한 요구가 확대될 가능성도 존재함. HBM4E와 커스텀 HBM 개발 
역시 고객사 일정에 맞춰 진행되고 있어 차세대 제품에서도 경쟁력을 유지할 것으로 기대됨. 
한편 2027년 CAPEX는 선단 공정 전환과 용인 클러스터를 비롯한 국내외 생산 인프라 확충으로 전년 대비 증가할 전망
임. 다만 CAPEX 확대를 과거와 같은 공급 과잉의 신호로 해석할 필요는 없다고 판단함. 선단 공정의 기술적 난도 상승
과 고부가 제품 비중 확대에 따라 투자금액 증가가 실제 생산량 증가로 정비례하지 않기 때문임. 특히 동사는 신규 팹 
건설 일정을 앞당기면서도 클린룸은 순차적으로 개방하고 실제 생산능력 확대는 최종 수요와 수익성을 고려해 단계적으
로 진행할 계획임. 고객사의 투자 계획뿐 아니라 재무건전성과 자금조달 여력, 공급망 준비 상황까지 점검해 CAPEX 집
행에 반영한다는 점도 긍정적임. 이는 AI 인프라 투자 확대에 대응하면서도 과거 메모리 산업의 공급 과잉 위험을 최소
화하기 위한 전략으로 판단함. 
동사는 이에 더해 2026년 회계연도 기준 약 100조원 규모의 주주환원 계획을 발표했음. 현금배당 30조원 이상, 자사주 
매입 및 소각 60조원 이상으로 구성되며, 기존에 약속했던 잉여현금흐름(FCF)의 50% 이상을 주주에게 환원한다는 원칙
을 구체화한 것임. 지난 8월 발표한 40조원 규모의 자사주 매입은 10월 21일 완료됐으며, 해당 주식은 향후 소각될 예정
임. 이번 3분기 이사회에서는 현금배당 14조원과 추가 자사주 매입 20조원을 결의했으며, 자사주 매입액 중 10조원은 전
량 소각하고 나머지 10조원은 임직원 보상에 활용할 계획임. 임직원 보상용 자사주는 주주환원 규모에서 제외됨. 특히 
향후에도 자사주 매입을 지속할 계획이라는 점에서 주주환원 정책의 지속성이 강화됐다는 판단임.

### Page 3

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

3 
3분기 주당 배당금은 2만원으로, 실적 개선을 반영한 일반배당 1만원과 키옥시아 투자수익을 활용한 특별배당 1만원으
로 구성됨. 총 배당 규모는 약 14조원이며 회사는 올해 실현한 키옥시아 투자수익의 대부분을 3분기 및 연말 특별배당을 
통해 주주에게 환원할 계획임. 이번 결정은 메모리 가격 상승과 고부가 제품 확대에 따른 현금창출력 개선이 실질적인 
주주환원 확대로 연결되고 있음을 보여줌. 특히 2027년 설비투자 확대가 예상되는 상황에서도 대규모 현금배당과 자사
주 소각을 병행한다는 점은 재무적 여력에 대한 자신감을 반영하는 것으로 판단됨. 향후에도 안정적인 실적 성장과 FCF 
확대가 이어질 경우 주주환원의 지속 가능성이 높아질 전망이며 이는 과거 대비 높아진 이익 창출력과 함께 중장기 밸류
에이션 재평가를 뒷받침하는 요인으로 작용할 것으로 예상됨. 
결국 이번 실적발표에서 확인된 핵심은 예상보다 강한 메모리 가격 상승과 HBM4의 본격적인 출하 확대, 그리고 장기계
약을 통한 이익 가시성 개선임. 특히 HBM4의 높은 가격과 안정적인 양산 수율, 기존 DRAM의 가격 상승이 동시에 확
인되면서 2027년 이익 추정치의 추가 상향 가능성이 높아졌다고 판단함. 여기에 LTA 확대에 따른 중장기 이익 안정성 
개선과 대규모 주주환원까지 더해지고 있음. 실적 추정치 상향과 이익의 지속성 개선이 동시에 진행되고 있다는 점에서 
긍정적인 투자 시각을 유지함.

합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 5

SK하이닉스 (000660) [한화리서치]

5 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 09 월 30 일) 
투자등급 매수 중립 매도 합계

3Q26Re: 신규 시설 매출 인식 개시 
연결 기준 매출액 1조 4,903억원(+18.5%YoY, 이하 YoY 생략), 영업이익 6,401억원
(+1.0%, OPM 42.9%)로 컨센서스를 상회하는 실적 기록. 1~4공장이 완전 가동을 유지하는 
한편, 5공장은 PPQ 배치 생산 본격화되면서 매출 기여 확대. 상업 생산 전환이 예상되는 
2H27부터 이익 기여 커질 것으로 기대. 
연초 인수한 Rockville 공장은 3Q에도 안정적인 상업 생산을 이어갔으며 2Q 생산 물량의 
매출이 인식되며 매출 성장에 기여. 동사는 Rockville 공장 매출 인식 등을 반영하여 기존 
+15~20% 성장에서 +20%로 연간 가이던스 상향.   
고객사 수주 회복 기대 
3Q26 기준 누적 수주액은 219억 달러로 전분기 대비 소폭 증가했으나 과거 연간 수주액 
증가 대비 부족한 수준. 저조한 수주 실적은 지난해 초 의약품에 대한 관세 불확실성 증가로 
고객사들의 수주 문의가 저조했던 영향. 26년 초부터 관세 불확실성이 낮아지며 수주 문의
는 회복 추세. 약 1년 정도 논의 기간이 소요되는 것을 고려할 때 26년말~27년 초 수주 회
복 확인 가능할 것으로 기대. 
안정적인 공급망 유지가 중요한 CDMO 산업 특성상 노조 파업 가능성도 고객사 수주를 늦
추는 요인으로 판단. 다만, 10/16 인천 고등법원이 쟁의행위 가처분 항고심에 대해 인용 결
정을 하며 기존 3개 공정에서 9개 공정으로 확대되며 파업 우려는 다소 완화. 
투자 의견 Buy, 목표 주가 200만원 유지 
매수 의견과 목표 주가 200만원을 유지. 원달러 약세에도 불구하고 5공장 Ramp-up과 
Rockville 공장 매출 기여로 견조한 실적 성장 지속될 것으로 예상. 파업 이슈 해소와 대형 
수주 계약이 주가 상승을 이끌 것으로 판단.

### Page 1

2026.10.29   
HD현대중공업  (329180)

3Q26: 조선사는 우직해야 한다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 5조 7,536억원/영업이익 8,992억원 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 매출은 5조 7,536억원/영업이익은 8,992억원으로 
컨센서스(6조 1,295억원/9,671억원) 및 당사 추정치(6조 859억원/9,679억원) 대비 
매출 및 영업이익 각각 -6.1%/-7.0%, -5.5%/-7.1%를 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다.  
조선사업부 매출이 4조 3,988억원/영업이익 6,463억원(OPM 14.7%)이며 이중 상선 
OPM은 14.7%로, 2분기 17.8%에 비해 3.1%P 하락하였으나 1분기 15.9%와 크게 
차이나지 않는 수준을 유지하면서 조업일수 및 환율 하락에도 불구하고 P-MIX및 
빈티지 개선에 따른 효과를 보여주었다. 해양사업부는 매출 3,799억원/영업이익 
1,265억원(OPM 33.3%)로 지난 분기(15.9%)보다도 훨씬 높은 영업이익을 
기록하였다. 엔진기계사업부는 매출 9,511억원/영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)를 
기록하며 역시 견조한 실적을 기록하였다.  
일회성 이익으로 Trion 공사의 C/O 180억원, 일회성 손실로는 셧다운에 따른 
조업감소 영향 500억원 및 환율하락에 따른 이익 감소분 450억원이 있었으며 금액은 
공개되지 않았으나 임단협 잠정타결에 따른 성과급/타결금 지급에 대한 추가 충당금의 
75% 인식이 반영되었다. 알려진 일회성만 제거하더라도 실질 영업이익률은 
17%이상으로 2분기 16.4%보다 높아진 호실적이라고 할 수 있다. 특히 RUYA 공사가 
본 궤도에 오르면서 해양사업부의 이익률이 괄목할 만큼 높아졌으며 지속가능하다는 
회사의 설명이다.

투자의견 BUY 및 조선업종 TOP PICK 유지, 목표주가 530,000원 하향 
HD현대중공업의 목표주가를 530,000원으로 38.3% 하향하지만, 투자의견 BUY와 
조선업종 TOP PICK을 유지한다. 환율 하락 및 28년 하반기 엔진증설 매출확대, 
해양공사 이익률 상승 등 요인을 종합 반영하여 실적 추정치를 미세 반영하여 도출한 
2027F BPS 131,910원에 Target P/B를 기존대비 하향한 4.0배로 적용하였다. 특수선 
기대감 및 선가상승 기대감 하락에 따른 조선업종 전반의 밸류에이션 하락을 반영하여 
Target을 낮췄지만 그럼에도 조선업종 중 가장 높은 멀티플을 부여하며 Top 
Pick의견을 견지한다. 여러 악재에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업은 탄탄하다. 본업인 
상선사업부의 9월말 기준 수주량은 137.9억 달러로 목표치를 이미 초과 달성하였으며 
전사 기준으로도 수주목표 90.5%를 채워넣었다. 수주잔고 또한 상선 421억 
달러/전사 609억 달러로 각각 작년 말 대비 12.6%/13.2% 증가하였다. 해양부문과 
엔진기계부문은 조선사업부보다도 높은 이익률로 든든한 실적의 기반이 되고 있다.  
최근 HD현대그룹은 미국의 EPC강자 Kiewit과 미국 합작법인 설립에 관한 계약을 
맺고 그린필드 기반의 미국 신규 조선소 건설을 추진하고 있다. 빠르면 올해 중으로 
건립과 관련한 세부 사항이 공개될 예정이며 HD현대중공업이 중추적인 역할을 담당할 
것으로 보인다. 일찍부터 브라운필드 방식으로 접근한 동종사와 달리 다소 시간은 
걸렸지만 과감하게 결정된 투자다. 조선소 예상 준공 역시 2028년 이후로 예상되어 
당장 기업가치에 반영하기는 어려우나 엔진 증설과 함께 2028년 후에는 분명한 실적 
상향 요인이 될 것으로 예상한다. 우직하게 본업에 충실하며 신중하게 미래를 준비하는 
조선소, 귀감이다.  
Buy (Maintain)

## Document 276: [373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[373220] LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적LG에너지솔루션(373220) 보상금 제외해도 호실적보상금 제외해도 호실적 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-12
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

(373220) 
LG 에너지솔루션 
2026.10.12

에너지/인프라/배터리 황성현, Ph.D. 
6878 / tjdgus2009@eugenefn.com

보상금 제외해도 호실적 
 투자의견 BUY(유지) 
 목표주가 530,000 원(상향) 
 현재주가 401,000 원(10/8)

 [투자의견] 당사는 LG에너지솔루션에 대해 실적 추정치 상향을 반영하여 목
표주가를 530,000원으로 13% 상향하고, 투자의견 BUY를 유지한다. 
3Q26부터 ESS 물량 증가에 따른 가동률 상승 효과가 본격적으로 실적에 
반영되기 시작했으며, 4Q26에도 AMPC 제외 흑자 기조를 이어갈 전망이
다. 전사 가동률은 3분기 65%까지 상승한 것으로 파악되며, 이에 따른 펀
더멘털 개선이 본격화되고 있다고 판단한다. 
 [3Q26 잠정 실적 Review] 매출액 9.6조원(+28%qoq, +59%yoy), 영업이
익 7,560억원(+567%qoq, +26%yoy)을 기록하며 당사 추정을 큰 폭으로 
상회했다. 북미 생산보조금(AMPC)을 제외한 매출액은 9.2조원, 영업이익은 
3,391억원(영업이익률 3.7%)으로, 2분기(-1,277억원, -1.7%) 대비 흑자 
전환에 성공했다. ① 원통형은 테슬라향 물량 호조에 힘입어 영업이익 
2,010억원(영업이익률 8.1%)을 달성한 것으로 추정된다. ② EV는 유럽 폴
란드 공장의 가동률 상승과 GM 얼티엄의 8월 가동 개시로 매출이 증가했
다. 여기에 완성차 업체 보상금 3,000억원이 반영되며 영업이익 1,090억
원(영업이익률 2.5%)을 기록한 것으로 추정된다. ③ ESS는 매출액이 50% 
이상 급증하며 실적 서프라이즈를 견인했다. AMPC 포함 영업이익은 4,460
억원(영업이익률 16.0%)으로 추정된다. 2분기 매출 성장률 +17%qoq에 
이어 3분기 성장세가 더욱 가팔라졌다는 점이 긍정적이다. 
 [4Q26 Preview] 매출액 8.0조원(-17%qoq, +30%yoy), 영업이익 6,790
억원(-10%qoq, 흑전yoy)으로 추정한다. 4분기 실적은 ESS 수익성 개선 
속도와 완성차 업체 보상금 반영 규모에 따라 변동할 전망이다. 동사는 기존 
가이던스를 통해 하반기 북미 ESS 생산량이 상반기 대비 2배 증가할 것으
로 제시한 바 있다. 3분기 ESS는 AMPC를 제외하더라도 양호한 수익성을 
기록한 것으로 추정된다. 향후 팩 생산 병목 해소와 북미 공장 가동 안정화
에 따른 고정비 부담 완화가 이어지며 추가적인 손익 개선이 가능할 전망이
다. EV 역시 GM 얼티엄 생산 확대와 유럽 공장 가동률 상승이 지속되며 
AMPC 제외 적자 규모를 점진적으로 축소할 것으로 예상한다. 
 [투자포인트] 9월 미시간 랜싱 공장의 개소식이 개최됐다. 랜싱 공장은 당초 
EV용 파우치 배터리 생산거점으로 계획됐으나 ESS 중심으로 역할이 재편
됐으며, 내년에는 10GWh 이상의 각형 LFP 생산라인이 추가될 예정이다. 
2027년에는 신규 대규모 증설보다 기존 북미 생산거점의 가동률 상승과 
ESS 생산량 확대를 통한 수익성 개선이 핵심이 될 전망이다. 북미 전력망 
접속 지연과 AI 데이터센터의 부하 변동성 확대에 따라 Stand-alone ESS, 
BTM, UPS, BBU 수요가 증가하면서 신규 수주도 지속 확대될 것으로 예상
한다. 특히 동사의 Non-PFE 요건 충족과 SI· O&M을 아우르는 End-to-
End 솔루션 역량이 북미 ESS 시장에서 경쟁력으로 작용할 전망이다.

주: 2024~2025 년 영업이익은 AMPC 회계 기준 변경 전 수치

Compliance Notice 
당사는 자료 작성일 기준으로 지난 3 개월 간 해당종목에 대해서 유가증권 발행에 참여한 적이 없습니다 
당사는 본 자료 발간일을 기준으로 해당종목의 주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다  
당사는 동 자료를 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다 
조사분석담당자는 자료작성일 현재 동 종목과 관련하여 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 조사분석담당자 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다 
동 자료는 당사의 제작물로서 모든 저작권은 당사에게 있습니다 
동 자료는 당사의 동의 없이 어떠한 경우에도 어떠한 형태로든 복제, 배포, 전송, 변형, 대여할 수 없습니다 
동 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료 및 정보로부터 얻어진 것이나, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 어떠
한 경우에 동 자료는 고객의 주식투자의 결과에 대한 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙자료로 사용될 수 없습니다

## Document 289: An RBA peak of greater conviction (2026-11-18)

Source PDF: all report pdf/An RBA peak of greater conviction (2026-11-18).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#australia #luciellis #rba
An RBA peak of greater conviction
By Luci Ellis
Chief Economist Westpac Group, Westpac
08:56 November 13 2026 Share
We already believed the RBA cash rate was likely at a peak, even if RBA officials were less
convinced. A deeper housing downturn and early signs of cracks in the consumer sector
point to an even softer 2027. Fading inflation risks from oil also boost our conviction on
rates being at a peak.

### Page 2

It was already our view that the RBA was likely done hiking the cash rate after the November
increase. Since then, our conviction on that call has strengthened. Later today we will publish
the November Market Outlook with revised forecasts. GDP growth, the labour market and housing
market have all been revised down in 2027. Underlying inflation has been revised down slightly in the
second half of 2027, bringing the year-ended figure for calendar 2027 more decisively inside the

RBA’s 2–3% target range than our previous forecast round. The unemployment rate forecast has
been revised up to peak above 5%. Based on this revised forecast profile, an increase in the cash
rate from here would be unlikely and – we would argue – undesirable.
A range of considerations underlie this forecast revision. The housing market has undershot our
forecasts over the past couple of months. Turnover and financing activity have been coming in
under our earlier expectations. Mortgage loan inquiries have taken another leg down in October after

the September rate hike, after finding something of a level in prior months, with the additional impact
of the November rate hike still to come.
We are also seeing early signs of a turn in the consumer. Consumer income and spending have
so far been quite resilient to pressures from fuel prices and rising interest rates. But our Westpac
Card Tracker is starting to show that discretionary services spending is turning down again in Q4,
joining the discretionary goods category that was already quite weak. We would not over-emphasise

this point, but it does imply that the risks of further surprising resilience are fading.
Subsequent to the November Monetary Policy Board (MPB) meeting, business survey data
has shown weaker conditions and a sharp decline in forward orders. They also show further
evidence that current inflation is mostly a non-labour cost shock that is not being fully
passed through to consumer prices.
As we noted following the November MPB meeting, oil and fuel prices are already materially

lower than the track assumed in the RBA’s forecasts. Dated Brent is nearly one-fourth lower than
on 28 October, the date the RBA’s forecasts were finalised, and spot Brent is around 15% lower. This
significantly lowers the risk of a new wave of pass-through of fuel costs to other prices, as well as
easing consumer inflation perceptions and expectations.
In the media conference following the November meeting, Governor Bullock flagged that it was a

good time to “take stock” of the policy tightening to date. Both we and the market read this as a
signal that the MPB would pause at the December meeting. The 6:3 split vote at the meeting also
suggests that some external members are more attentive to downside risks than the internal
members.
A rate hike in February 2027 cannot be ruled out but it is looking even less likely given signs
in the data and surveys. The MPB will at least discuss a hike in February. The staff and senior

officials remain sensitive to inflation risks and have a downbeat view of supply capacity growth.
Given what we are seeing in the economy now, though, a rate hike in 2027 would require
either a material new inflation shock that has not yet occurred, or would constitute a policy
error on the part of the RBA (something that we will never forecast as a base case, knowing the
talents and capabilities of the staff and the Board). We concur with the dissenting voters in the MPB

that the downside risks need more attention. Policy is clearly restrictive, and the risk of overdoing the
inflation correction is growing. And by the time we get to February, we think there will be more
evidence that the domestic economy is slowing materially, and the inflation outlook with it.

### Page 3

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Oil Price Dynamics Remain Key to the Outlook
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
Inflation, interest rates and equity market dynamics have changed materially since mid-
August, as widening attacks on Middle East and Russian crude oil and refining capacity
worsened supply and price conditions. Indeed, with the oil market already strained, an
early-September attack on a major Saudi Arabian export pipeline sharply raised concerns

about a protracted and deepening oil crisis. While Middle East crude exports eventually
managed to significantly rebound, increased security risks and freight costs raised
physical crude prices.
Against this backdrop, the spot Brent price surged from $92/barrel in mid-August
back into a $115 to $135/barrel price range until recently. Reflecting unusual market
tightness, other North Sea oil grades were recently trading even higher, up to $160/
barrel, according to the International Energy Agency (IEA). Even as it cooled from $135

to $115/barrel between October 2 and October 20, spot Brent is still almost double
year-ago levels, and so are many diesel prices. The AAA average U.S. retail diesel price
recently hit a new record and was 74% higher year-over-year (YoY) as of October 20.
With insufficient regional supply, the New York Harbor wholesale diesel price is 130%
higher from a year ago, according to the Energy Information Administration (EIA).
Indeed, sharply higher oil prices and a growing diesel shortage have put the global diesel

market under extreme strain. The U.S., for example, has little distillate cushion and
limited spare refining capacity. The distillate inventory (diesel and fuel oil) has decreased
from 37 days of demand coverage in January to just 26 days by early October, according
to the EIA. This 30% drop brought the supply buffer to the bottom of its historical
range. With limited refining capacity and reliance on imports for about 10% of regional
consumption, East Coast distillate inventories are the lowest since 1990, for example,

and are expected to remain below their 5-year average into late 2027, according to EIA
data. More broadly, according to the IEA’s October Oil Market Report (OMR), “oil market
balances are forecast to remain in deficit through the end of the year, with constrained
middle distillate supplies likely to persist well into 2027.”
Thus, although wage growth/labor demand do not appear inflationary, the oil crisis
risks continuing to feed “core” inflation. According to the Federal Reserve’s Beige Book1

covering precisely the mid-August to early-October period of heightened oil-market
turmoil, “higher oil prices permeated all District reports,” with firms noting that fuel
surcharges were passing on increased costs throughout the supply chain. The 22% rise
in airfares over the past year alone contributed around 0.2 percentage point (pp) to the
2.6% 12-month increase in the “core” consumer price index (CPI) for September. Aside
from transportation costs effects, the spike in diesel prices also portends high food

prices with a few-month lag given planting/harvesting cost considerations.
By solidifying expectations for higher Fed interest rates to fight inflation, the surge in oil
prices has contributed to the spike in long-term Treasury yields this year. According to an
October 13, 2026, Empirical Research Partners report, the Iran-war oil crisis is estimated
to have contributed about 0.50 pp to the roughly 2.40 pp increase in the real U.S. long-
term Treasury rate from 2020 to early October (Exhibit 1A). According to the report, this

oil-price effect accounts for “much of the rise in real rates since February 27, 2026...”
On the positive side, while most other underpinnings for higher rates compared to the
pre-pandemic decade are structural (Exhibit 1A), the oil-price-related effect on interest
rates is reversible, implying that a resolution to the current oil crisis has the potential to
help lower both inflation and interest rates, relieving some of the current headwinds to
growth and asset prices.

Indeed, while much of the increase in interest rates since the pandemic reflects stronger,
more normal economic growth, and has thus been accompanied by surging corporate
1 Summary of Commentary on Current Economic Conditions by Federal Reserve District, Federal Reserve, October 2026.
Investment Implications
Oil market conditions remain a swing
factor for the outlook. A shift lower in
oil risk premia and improvement on
the refining front would go a long way

toward easing inflation and interest
rates while bolstering risk-asset
appetite. A gradual increase in Fixed
Income portfolio duration continues to
make sense, in our view.

3 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
earnings and risk-asset prices, worsening oil-market conditions since mid-summer and
associated spike in yields have been accompanied by a loss of momentum in U.S. equity
prices (Exhibit 1B). Given their higher sensitivity to energy price and interest rates shocks,
Small-caps lost relatively more momentum than Large-caps during this period.
The Large-cap rally also faded, though, as oil and interest rate headwinds widened stock-

level return dispersion, with roughly 40% of constituent stocks in bear market territory
(down 20% or more). Still, buoyed by the Technology sector as well as by expectations
of conflict resolution, the market-capitalization weighted S&P 500 Index has mainly
consolidated just below its peak. While the equal-weighted S&P 500 Index stumbled more,
it was just about 3% off its peak as of October 20.
While the economy remains resilient and profit expectations remain strong, persistent

oil market turmoil makes the macro backdrop more fragile. For example, the Beige Book
not only highlights concern about spreading fuel-related inflation, as noted above, but its
language also suggests that economic activity lost some momentum since its mid-July
to late-August report. Overall, it describes growth as slight to moderate, with generally
moderate manufacturing growth except for robust activity in the energy-heavy Dallas
District. Little growth is reported in other sectors overall, with most Districts seeing little

to no change in consumer spending, for example.
Employment was described as stable to little changed, with generally adequate labor supply
except for certain specialized trade workers. Five of twelve Districts note some reports of
businesses cutting staff through attrition, delaying hiring or anticipating less job creation
as a result of AI-driven productivity gains. Most Districts reported that business maintained
a positive outlook for coming months, though “expectations tend to be for slight growth,

weighted toward manufacturing and burdened by persistent uncertainty.”
The September employment and retail sales reports seem to validate the anecdotal Beige
Book evidence. Also, the 0.4% annualized manufacturing production growth over the July to
September period (and 1.1% YoY, according to the Federal Reserve Broad), while welcome,
is hardly an indication of booming conditions. Against a backdrop of rising electricity prices,
wage growth lagging inflation, and higher gasoline prices broadly offsetting this year’s tax

cut benefits, real consumer spending is unlikely to accelerate much absent stronger labor
demand and real wage growth. A resolution to the oil crisis that brings economic relief and
tempers interest rates is thus critical for continued expansion in 2027, in our view.

Investment Implications
Portfolio maintenance is a key
component of successful investing.
It’s time for investors to assess their
portfolios—or look under the hood.
MARKET VIEW
Six Questions Investors Should Be Asking Themselves Right Now
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
After four years of rising equity prices, investors have plenty to celebrate. Despite

inflation scares, geopolitical tensions, trade wars, rising interest rates and breathless
predictions around AI, the S&P 500 has returned some 131% (total returns) since the
bull market in equities commenced in October 2022. Corporate earnings have generally
exceeded expectations; AI has ignited a historic investment boom, and America’s
technology giants have powered the market to new heights.
We believe the bull will rumble on into 2027. We also believe that now is the time for

investors to take stock of their portfolios, since bull markets have a way of changing the
composition of portfolios. In addition, investors should not assume that the investment
strategies that worked well in the past four years will necessarily deliver comparable
results during the next four. Below are six questions investors should be asking
themselves right now:
Number One: How concentrated is my portfolio? Many investors believe they are
invested in the stock market when, in fact, they are invested in a handful of companies—

specifically AI-related names that have soared and dominated the index over the past
few years. Information technology now accounts for roughly 40% of the market cap of
the S&P 500, while the top 10 companies represent a share of 38.4%.
As the Financial Times recently noted, U.S. equity and credit markets have become so
increasingly concentrated in AI-related groups that “more than two-thirds of companies
in the Russell 1000 index of large U.S. groups are linked to AI, either directly or

indirectly or because the technology is becoming embedded in their business models.”2
Meanwhile, Big Technology hyperscalers and companies that are beneficiaries of the AI
spending boom make up about 16% of the U.S. high-grade bond market after a huge
borrowing binge, making the grouping the largest in the investment-grade bond market.2
The upshot: Investors that have been rewarded by concentration need to ask themselves
what percentage of their wealth is tied to the all-encompassing AI trade and consider

what would happen if mega-cap tech leadership falters.
Number Two: How diversified is my portfolio? While concentration asks whether
investors own too much of a few assets, diversification asks whether investors own enough
of different assets that behave differently when economic cycles or financial conditions
change. A portfolio containing hundreds of U.S. Equities via exchange-traded funds, mutual
funds and other assets may appear diversified. But if those companies are overwhelmingly

exposed to the same economic cycle, interest rate environment, or technology theme,
the diversification benefits are diluted. Case in point: While we remain constructive on
Emerging Market (EM) Equities, investors need to understand that a significant driver of
EM returns has come from AI-driven markets like Taiwan and South Korea.
Today, true diversification means considering exposure to hard assets (commodities, real
estate), EM mining and agricultural leaders, EM bonds and currencies, China bio-technology,

Small-cap international, and Private Equity and Hedge Funds for qualified investors.
Number Three: How tax-efficient is my portfolio? Bull markets create wealth—in
addition to tax liabilities. The good news is that after four years of rising equity returns,
investors are sitting on sizable, unrealized capital gains. The bad news: Market gains
can feel like a financial straitjacket, making it difficult to rebalance portfolios without
triggering significant tax liabilities.

Against this backdrop, now is the time for investors to review where the assets are
held—distinguishing between taxable and tax-advantaged accounts, and the attendant tax
ramifications. The appropriate mix depends on individual circumstances, including future
2 “Investors seek shelter from heightened exposure to AI”, The Financial Times, October 6, 2026.

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
withdrawal taxes. It is also time to consider tax-loss strategies, charitable donations of
securities and other tactics and strategies since in the end, the measure of investment
success isn’t simply how much money investors make. It’s how much they keep.
Number Four: Is it time to take profits?* There is a peculiarity to bull markets: The
more money investors make, the more the attendant tax liabilities. Tax liabilities, in turn,

can discourage selling, and the reluctance to sell can leave investors dangerously exposed
to yesterday’s leaders and compound concentration risks. That brings up an emotionally
difficult decision: When should investors sell their leaders or take some chips off the table?
The question is notably relevant today, given four years of substantial Equity gains.
Beyond tax considerations, investors need to understand that profit-taking can create
financial flexibility. Realized after-tax gains can replenish cash reserves, fund near-

term spending needs, reduce leverage or most importantly, provide capital to rebalance
portfolios in other areas of the market. In general, the goal of taking profits isn’t to get
out of the market—it is to ensure that after years of accumulated gains, these proceeds
are redeployed and redistributed across various asset classes that continue to support
and underpin long-term wealth accumulation.
Number Five: How well is my portfolio positioned for a new interest rate

regime? For much of the past quarter century, investors operated in a world of copious
liquidity, declining interest rates and relatively cheap capital. The era of capital abundance
favored stocks over bonds, but that era is over—we are shifting to a world of capital
scarcity, or intense competition for capital and the attendant hike in the cost of capital.
In this world, higher rates weigh the most on long-duration assets. Positioning for this
regime favors companies with strong balance sheets and cash flow over firms with heavy

debt or refinancing needs. High-quality bonds are more attractive in this environment;
sovereign debt risks are concentrated more overseas (i.e., France) than the U.S.
Number Six: How well is my portfolio positioned for the next shock? Shocks
happen. If the past four years have taught investors anything, it is that the unexpected
should be the expected. This decade alone has produced gut-wrenching market moves
related to a global pandemic, a ground war in Europe, the reconfiguration of global

supply chains, epic technological change and a war in the Middle East. Through it all, U.S.
Equities have trended higher but hardly in a linear fashion.
The next shocks could emerge in the global bond market, where mounting government
debt and higher borrowing costs have triggered mounting volatility. It could emerge from
disappointing returns on massive AI investments. Or it could come from a geopolitical
event, another energy shock or any other unforeseen event. Investors can’t predict the

next shock, but they can prepare for it. So now is the time for investors to stress test
their portfolios against a potential shock, examining how a steep downdraft in Equities
or spike in bond yields would affect their retirement plans, liquidity needs and other
financial goals and objectives.
Exhibit 2: Six Questions to Stress Test Your Portfolio.
The six questions What investors should examine Portfolio diagnostic
1. How concentrated am I? Mega-cap technology exposure and overlapping holdings % of portfolio in top 10 stocks

2. Am I truly diversified? Asset allocation, geographic mix and correlated exposures Current equity/bond mix versus target
3. How tax-efficient am I? Unrealized gains, asset location and tax-loss harvesting Embedded capital gains and estimated tax cost of rebalancing
4. Is it time to take profits? Portfolio drift, oversized winners and valuations Positions exceeding target allocation
5. Am I ready for higher interest rates? Duration, leverage, refinancing risks and cash flows Bond duration and exposure to highly leveraged companies

6. Am I prepared for the next shock? Liquidity, downside exposure and portfolio resilience Estimated loss in a 20% equity selloff
Source: Chief Investment Office. Data as of October 22, 2026.
* “Pop the Champagne,” Portfolio Insights, Chief Investment Office, August 2026.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 26, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What are rising credit spreads signaling about the broader market environment?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Officer
Rising French bond yields with a sharp rise in spreads above German bunds, mixed
with AI buildout concerns, are creating nervousness across the capital markets. This
nervousness centers around the ability to fund deficits, the potential for slower growth

and the future return on the announced capital investment. We expect these concerns
to continue to linger well into next year as growth worries remain in France, bond
yields stay sticky around current levels, and, in the case of AI, investors demand more
compensation for financing.
At this stage, we do not expect the rising spreads in targeted areas to be a precursor
for a material slowdown in economic growth overall. Rather, we believe this is a natural

signal that investors are moving up the quality stack as we move into the middle of
the AI buildout. The bottom line is that rising credit spreads in lower quality corners of
the market are natural along the lifecycle of financing. When free cash flow is called
into question, or when debt financing costs begin to approach the expected return on
investment, investors begin to reprice risk.
We continue to emphasize higher quality balance sheets in corporate and sovereign

credit as well as higher quality leaders in the AI buildout overall. The closer we get to
2027, investors will likely begin to transition from too much exposure to the “buildout”
and not enough exposure to “AI adopters.”

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
SS&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and
services.
S&P 500 Market-cap Weighted Index is a float-adjusted market-capitalization-weighted index, meaning a company's influence and percentage weight in the index are determined by its
total market value (share price multiplied by the number of outstanding shares).

S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is a variation of the traditional S&P 500 where every single company in the index is given the exact same weight—0.2%—regardless of its size or market
capitalization.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Industrials Index imposes equal weights on the index constituents included in the S&P 500 that are classified in the GICS® industrials sector.
Russell 1000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of the 1,000 largest publicly traded companies in the United States.

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.

## Document 323: Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-18)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ the source and persistence of inflation (2026-11-18).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: the source and persistence of inflation
By Ryan Wells, Illiana Jain & Elliot Clarke
11:16 October 30 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia this week, all eyes were on the September CPI. Headline inflation rose 0.8% (4.3%yr) in
the month, largely owing to a 9.4% jump in fuel prices and a seasonal surge in domestic and
international holiday travel & accommodation. The underlying inflation pulse also proved hotter than

anticipated for Q3 overall, up 1.0% (3.7%yr).

### Page 2

While some parts of the basket are seeing disinflation – housing in particular looks to be cooling faster
than expected as builders lure sales with discounts – other components are still showing persistence.
Highlighting this is market services ex volatiles, which the RBA watches closely as an indicator of price
pressures related to domestic capacity. In Q3, this metric rose 1.2% (3.2%yr), and the monthly detail
points to momentum holding up – in line with our expectations for another chunky read in Q4.

With inflation running well above the RBA’s August forecasts, and some of the energy risks flagged
then materialising, we continue to expect the Monetary Policy Board will deliver a 25bp rate hike to
4.85% in November. Beyond November, another rate hike in February is a risk, but is not currently part
of our base case.
Across the Tasman, the RBNZ this week surprised market expectations for a 25bp rate hike, deciding
instead to wait for further information before taking the next step with policy. The decision was made

4:3, with the Governor in the ‘on hold’ camp. A 25bp hike in December to 3.00% and further tightening
in 2027 (Westpac NZ forecasts a peak of 4.00% in September) seems probable; however, each
decision will depend on fuel prices, inflation expectations and evidence of second-round pricing
pressures, along with the evolution of global economic risks.
In the US, the FOMC also held rates steady at its October meeting. Based on prior comments by key
members, the decision was not a surprise, and neither were Governor Cook and Cleveland Fed

President Hammack’s dissents in favour of a 25bp hike. The Committee remains positive on economic
activity, characterising it in the statement as “expanding at a strong pace” (an upgrade from “solid” in
September). Chair Warsh also subsequently emphasised the breadth of economic growth outside of
tech-related investment (evinced by Q3 GDP the day after; see below).
The Committee refrained from spotlighting key price risks in the statement. However, inflation “remains

elevated” and the FOMC are determined to achieve a “timely” and sustainable return of annual inflation
to 2.0%yr after 6 years above target. This guidance and the “accumulation of more information will help
the Committee determine the policy stance” suggests another 25bp hike is likely in December.
Thereafter, the FOMC will remain attentive to any broadening in inflation and related risks through
2027; although, in the absence of another shock, we expect them to remain on hold.

The Q3 GDP report certainly supported Chair Warsh’s view on the breadth of growth, the 3.4%
annualised gain for GDP benefitting from a 3.8% annualised increase in household consumption,
12.7% growth in non-residential investment as well as a robust increase in government spending.
Notably though, the contribution from business investment was effectively offset by net exports’ drag
given most of the componentry related to AI infrastructure investment is imported. This is not to say that

US firms won’t benefit from the investment once complete, but simply that much of the initial return
goes to the foreign businesses who provide the necessary capital equipment.
On consumption, the monthly PCE data implies downside risks remain for the consumer, the increase
in nominal spending seen in the month requiring dissaving and, on a real basis, consumption flat in the
month following a 0.5% gain in August. Cost-of-living pressures and uncertainty over the outlook is

### Page 3

Related articles
expected to see household consumption growth average around trend over the coming year, albeit with
considerable volatility quarter to quarter. This should see PCE inflation slow back towards its 2.0%yr
medium-term target as the FOMC desires, from 3.6%yr and 3.1%yr for the headline and core
measures respectively as of September.
Across the Atlantic, the ECB also left its key policy rates unchanged in October. Of note from the
meeting communications, energy prices continue to pose upside risks for inflation, but also downside

risks for growth. "While the full effects of the energy shock are still unfolding, underlying inflation has
remained broadly stable so far and wages do not show a material response". "Financing conditions
have [also] tightened", "which is likely to weigh on inflation and growth". In the press conference,
President Lagarde noted that, between the October and December meetings, a significant amount of
new economic information will be released, allowing forecasts and risks assessments to be tested.

Based on the October meeting communications, we continue to expect a rate hike at the December
meeting but believe this will be prove to be the last hike of the cycle. Note though, the spectrum of risks
Europe faces is broad and skewed towards inflation in the near term, hence the Council’s guidance will
remain hawkish. President Lagarde also announced today that she will step down as ECB President in
February ahead of the scheduled conclusion of her term next October.

The views expressed herein are based on current conditions, subject to change and may differ from other JPMorgan Chase & Co.

2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK

### Page 4

4
2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK
As we approach 2027, we are faced with two competing trends. Capital, as defined by
interest rates, is delivering higher base returns, so our expectations for required returns to
invest have risen. At the same time the need for this capital continues to grow to deliver the
opportunities, and support the requirements, on the horizon. Companies and governments
are looking to investors to fund the opportunities and priorities ahead, at a time when capital

is more expensive and investment decisions must clear a higher bar.
In last year’s Outlook, we explored how three forces, artificial intelligence (AI), global
fragmentation and inflation, were defining a new market frontier. In 2027, that frontier must
be funded.
Companies need capital to realize the full potential of AI. Governments need capital to
strengthen security and resilience in a fragmented world. At the same time, inflation,
economic growth and central bank policy continue to shape the investment environment.

With cash earning a credible return, investors can afford to be discerning about where they
put capital to work.
That is especially true of the AI build-out. Markets are beginning to question whether the
scale of investment will ultimately pay off. We believe AI is a transformative technology
that will fundamentally change how we live and work. We also believe investors need to be
selective, with a focus on the companies that will define this new frontier.

In this environment, portfolio diversification remains critical. Forward-looking returns for
stock-bond portfolios are the most attractive in years, but market volatility creates a wide
range of possible outcomes. A diversified portfolio and a long-term perspective can help
investors navigate both.
The new market frontier is starting to take shape. As investors consider the opportunities
ahead, we are proud to serve as your financial partner and remain committed to helping you

achieve your long-term goals.
Foreword
David Frame
CEO,  J.P. Morgan Global Private Bank

What we got Assessment
Three forces are reshaping the investment
landscape: artificial intelligence (AI), global

Scorecard for the 2026 Outlook

### Page 6

6
2027 ANNUAL OUTLOOK
In last year’s Outlook, we highlighted their Promise and Pressure,
and since then the salience of these themes has only grown.
AI offered the potential for higher corporate profits and faster
productivity growth, but also the risk of overinvestment,
exuberance and disruption. Fragmentation favored resilience and
security, but at the expense of cost, efficiency and government
budgets. Inflation had retreated from its post-pandemic peak but

seemed structurally higher and more vulnerable to rolling shocks.
Through 2026, markets moved to reflect the world we envisioned,
in some cases more emphatically than we expected.
Demand for AI continued to far outstrip the supply of AI
computing power (“compute” in the tech sector’s shorthand).
This has rewarded the companies designing, producing and
providing the semiconductors, components and power required
to deploy AI at scale. AI model providers generated real revenue

as businesses began paying up for AI-driven productivity gains.
Companies viewed as vulnerable to disruption underperformed.
Conflicts in Iran and Ukraine continued to highlight deep
vulnerabilities in energy supply chains and defense inventories.
The companies that we believe strengthen economic and national
security resilience outperformed even as traditional defense
companies lagged. Inflation remained above central bank targets,
bond yields climbed to multi-decade highs and a global rate-hiking

cycle kicked into gear.
Against that backdrop, a traditional balanced portfolio seems
set to deliver high single-digit returns. Real assets (such as
infrastructure and commodities) and active strategies (such
as diversified hedge funds) have earned their keep as portfolio
diversifiers.
The three forces (AI, fragmentation, inflation) continue to define
the economic and market environment. And they’re interacting
in sometimes unexpected ways that investors need to understand.

One theme links the three: An enormous demand for capital in both
the private and public sectors is raising the cost of capital. (It’s the
law of supply and demand we remember from Economics 101.)
Investors like you set the price for that capital. That is a position
of strength. It also compels decisions that cannot be deferred.
As many investors look to protect—as well as expand upon—several
years of handsome equity market gains, those decisions become

even more important.
Technology companies need your capital—trillions of dollars’
worth—to fund the next phase of the AI build-out. Governments
need it to finance security, resilience and rearmament initiatives.
Consumers, companies and sovereigns all need it to adapt to an
inflationary, fragmented world.
But inflation, resilient economic growth and central bank policy
mean that cash is more attractive. Today, it earns a real return.
Every financing decision, from a hyperscaler’s data center to a

government’s defense budget to your own portfolio allocation,
now carries an opportunity cost that was obscured for most of
the last cycle.
The AI boom will continue only if investors like you are willing
to provide the financing to bridge the gap between infrastructure
spending and consistent revenue. You will share the cost of
security with governments through taxes, inflation or both, but
you can also decide to own the beneficiaries of that investment.

And a higher cost of capital means every one of these decisions
must now clear a higher bar.
The old investing playbook may not power your portfolio through
the current cycle, even if forward looking returns for traditional
stock-bond mixes are still attractive. In the post–global financial
crisis cycle, policymakers kept the cost of capital low to incentivize
risk taking and boost asset prices. Today, capital has a price. We
believe there is a wider range of possible outcomes and, in the

context of public markets that have rarely been this concentrated,
an increased need to consider exposures you may not have.
Capital is the bridge to a world that is more secure and
productive. Your capital sets the price of progress. Place it wisely.

### Page 11

INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL11
In 2027, central banks will matter again. Before you place your capital, you must make a
judgment about how far central bank policy rates will rise. Inflation is key to that decision
for central banks—and for your portfolio.
We believe that the Federal Reserve and other global central banks are raising rates to
adjust their policy stance and influence inflation expectations. They are not looking to

lower demand. If we are right, the expansion can continue, and our constructive view
on multi-asset portfolios will hold. If we are wrong, the cycle could end with a hiking
campaign that breaks economic demand and pressures risk assets.
While U.S. inflation has remained above the Fed’s 2% target, that reflects supply-driven
factors such as energy prices. The labor market is stable and wage growth is decelerating.
Thus, we believe that inflation will begin to moderate. We see no sign of a nascent

wage-price spiral or overheating economy that the Fed would need to correct with an
aggressive rate hiking campaign.
The European Central Bank (ECB) seems to be running a similar playbook, signaling it will
continue hiking only if it sees further effects of higher energy prices. On the other hand,
the People’s Bank of China (PBoC), facing persistent disinflation, seems likely to keep
policy accommodative for the foreseeable future.
Last year, we argued that we are in a new

regime where transformative technologies
and a changing geopolitical environment
drive investment returns more than
traditional forces such as GDP growth
or central bank policies.

### Page 13

INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL
13
Inflation containment: Cost of
capital, not the policy rate, is doing
the heavy lifting
In the post–global financial crisis period, unprecedented monetary
policy (zero and negative interest rates and quantitative easing)
coupled with robust forward guidance from the Fed anchored
sovereign rates and compressed credit spreads and equity risk
premiums. In other words, capital had an “artificial” price. Today,

after substantial interest rate hikes, quantitative tightening and
the discontinuation of forward guidance, the price of capital is
more real.
Investors have as much to do with that change as central bankers.
Even before September, when the Fed raised its policy rate 25
basis points (bps), yields were already on the rise. Year to date the
10-year U.S. Treasury has risen roughly 75bps to around 5%. Fifty
of those basis points arrived in September alone, placing the move

in the top decile of monthly increases since 1962. Credit spreads
have widened modestly, investment grade (IG) by around 10bps,
high yield by roughly 40bps.
Importantly, markets are expecting more than two additional
hikes in the current cycle (the next one in December). That means
a higher cost of capital already constrains growth and inflation,
even absent an aggressive Fed.
What are the risks to our view?
What might compel the Fed to kick off an aggressive hiking cycle?

A stronger-than-expected economy, with wages accelerating
ahead of productivity, could force the Fed’s hand. So too could a
sustained move in 5-year/5-year inflation breakevens above 3%.
Indeed, the trajectory of data center construction is important
for bond yields. Ten-year U.S. Treasury yields peaked as investors
began to question further AI capex in 2027. A reacceleration in AI
infrastructure investment could push yields higher.
If higher diesel prices depress economic demand, it would present

a particular challenge to policymakers. In the United States, the
cost of diesel has risen to nearly $6.30/gallon from ~$3.80 before
the Middle East conflict began. Diesel at $10/gallon for a quarter
could make a meaningful dent in economic activity. The Fed would
then have to choose between hiking into a weakening economy
or risking that inflation expectations move higher. This is just one
example of the potential that the next growth downturn could be

accompanied by stubborn inflation, which would pose a challenge
for more traditional portfolios.
INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL

### Page 14

INFLATION, CENTRAL BANKS AND THE COST OF CAPITAL
Yes, inflation is still a threat
to your capital
Inflation is the foundation for our economic outlook, and we
believe it should also be reflected in the foundation of your
investment plan. Even though we expect inflation to moderate
through 2027, it still poses a key risk to the purchasing power
of your capital.
Many of our clients may also need to use a different benchmark
than policymakers and economists. From 2019 through 2025, the

headline PCE index rose at a ~3.5% annual rate. Over the same
period, based on our own clients’ spending data, average ticket
sizes on luxury goods purchases increased at more than a 6%
rate. Maintaining purchasing power for the goods and services
that matter most to you likely requires a higher hurdle than the
headline inflation index would suggest.
